**OWNER ANSWERS to this design (2026-10-03).**

- Q1 Hide: **stays hidden** until shown again (Show call, History row click, row scroll gesture); one small unsaved engine rule, as recommended.
- Q2 Defaults vs the person's changes: **the person's arrangement stays**; updates change only defaults never touched (after any drag, every position counts as theirs; sizes per panel).
- Q3 What 'left' means: "It depends completely on what the developer sets in settings when creating the panel." So placement must offer EXPLICIT values for both meanings (the whole row's edge, before fixed columns; and first/last among movable panels, after them) plus anchoring to a named panel; no implicit reading of a bare 'left'. The spec names the values.
- PRINCIPLE (owner, on Q4): "any panel behavior at all needs to be definable in the settings of that panel". Every panel behaviour is a SETTING with a library default, overridable by cascade (library -> app/workspace -> template -> instance); phonux defines the full catalog, enforces it safely and documents the defaults; it does not pick policy. Q1-Q3 answers become DEFAULTS (hide: sticky; rememberLayout: true; placement explicit). Q4 becomes a setting (toggleWhenClosed: 'ignore' | 'reopen', default ignore). The next revision adds a complete behaviour-settings catalog covering every engine behaviour.
- Q5 Events: **not yet**. This release has no 'something happened' events; the app reads the current panel list and observes when it changes. The design's events phase (L5) is dropped.
- Q6 (rev 3) keepMounted and look constants: **both now**. keepMounted is a per-panel setting in this release (its own queue entry plus a rendering-risk gate), and the row gap, drag-to-close distances, slide timing and hop rule become a `tuning` group in the workspace settings.
- Q7 (rev 3) Reset: **reset call only**. A `resetLayout` operation (one panel or all) the app can wire to a button; the `layoutEpoch` setting is DROPPED (41 settings + tuning).
- Q8 (rev 3) Changing settings after add: **any setting, later**. A `configure` call can change EVERY setting of a live panel; each setting change needs a defined transition rule (re-resolve through the cascade, re-check the row, and say what happens to a panel that is parked, hidden, protected or mid-operation), enforced and tested.
- Q9 (rev 4) keepMounted cap: **no cap**. The developer chooses per panel; phonux documents the memory cost and a hidden view can pause itself (usePanelHidden).
- Q10 (rev 4) Does a configure() change survive a restart: **runtime panels only**. Panels the code declares at startup come back as declared; panels created later restore exactly as configured. The person's own arrangement is saved regardless (Q2).
- Q11 (rev 4) Where template rules and workspace tuning change at runtime: **separate calls**: configure(panelId), configureTemplate(templateId), configureTuning(); a key sent to the wrong call is refused (wrong-scope).
- Q12 (rev 4) Pin/unpin (parkable and placement) at runtime, including on a hidden panel: "Pinning is just a setting toggle. let the dev decide where and how. For our phonux-app, pinning is currently a top right header button that should be defineable in the panel spawning script for it to be shown." So: (a) what a pin/unpin does to a hidden or parked panel is itself a SETTING the developer picks (e.g. refuse | show | apply-on-next-show), with a library default; (b) built-in HEADER CONTROLS (the pin button top-right, and the others the engine draws) are settings declared at spawn, e.g. headerControls: { pin: true }, shown only when set. REVISION 5 must add both; the rev-4 rule 'parkable flips only on a live panel' becomes that setting's default, not a hard rule.
- Q13 (rev 5) Tuning keys: "All eight": the four the owner named plus save delay, wheel pause and the two equal-priority tie-breaks, each defaulting to today's value.
- Q14 (rev 5) Header buttons, verbatim: "The button slots should be generic, where the developer chooses the icon and function that is run on click. For the app, we have a pin button and resize button."
- Q15 (rev 5) What happens when the row is full and every other panel is shielded, verbatim: "With horizontal scrolling its okay if a fixed panel scrolls off screen. A user may have 5 pinned panels, history, and 5 more closeable panels, but only sees 5 at a time. If browsing pinned panels and the user opens a new closeable panel, it should scroll to the newly opened panel."
- Q16 (rev 6) Default when a panel opens and the row is wider than the window: "Scroll for every panel" (every open panel stays in the row, the row scrolls and opening scrolls to the new panel; nothing is hidden for lack of room; a developer can still set 'park' on a panel). This CONFIRMS rev 6's overflow 'scroll' default, which is now the owner's decision, not an inference.
- Q17 (rev 6) The app's 'Max panels on screen' setting: "Retire it" (the window is as wide as the app window; the Settings section is removed).
- Q18 (rev 6) Mouse-only navigation along the row, verbatim: "We will implement a custom scroll bar in the future". So: this release keeps today's stepping (swipe, Shift + wheel, arrow keys, History click), no scrollbar now; the design must not preclude a later custom scrollbar (say what hook or setting it would use) and lists it as a deferred item, not an entry.
- Q19 (rev 6) Scroll the row while a dragged panel is held near the window's edge: "Yes, scroll at the edge". A per-panel setting (switchable per panel, with a library default) plus its own library entry; the release candidate waits for it. Specify edge zone, speed, interaction with drag-to-close, reorder hop rule, reduced motion, and the scroll-cancel rules of 6B.
- Q20 (rev 7) What a person sees when the app opens (the row runs pinned panels, History, closable panels, left to right): "Right end, newest first". So phonux-app sets `tuning.initialScroll: 'end'`: the newest closable panels are in view at launch; pinned panels and History start off screen to the left. The library default stays 'start'.
- Q21 (rev 7) The design-chosen scrolling defaults (`tuning.edgeScroll` 48 px zone, 900 px/s, 500 ms reduced-motion step; `scrollOnOpen` 'into-view'; `scrollOnMove` 'follow'): "Keep all of them". These are now owner-confirmed defaults. `revealOnScroll` 'edges' and the library's `tuning.initialScroll` 'start' stay design defaults the owner did not rule on.

# phonux next release: spawn a workspace, add panels, manage them safely, every behaviour a setting

Revision 7, final: revision 6 plus owner answers Q16-Q21, the rebase onto phonux-lib main 7a60e82 (PR #3 removed the 22 deprecated aliases and the two internal entry points; PR #4 hid ten values and three types from the barrel), every rev-6 and rev-7 plan-audit finding and the rev-7 review. Read-only: nothing was run, so each 'by reading' claim is pinned by a test in the entry that changes it. Citations: phonux-lib code at 7a60e82; `app:` is phonUX main 810cfb7. Each setting's row and `on_configure` rule are in the catalog (section 4, Appendix A); each has one owning entry (section 12).

## What changed in revision 7
- Q16: `overflow` 'scroll' is the owner's decision for every panel (decision 10); 'park' stays a per-panel opt-in.
- Q17: 'Max panels on screen' is retired. App (entry `app-retire-max-panels-on-screen`): Settings section, store, IPC and preload member deleted; the stored file is ignored and removed at launch by a new electron-free module. Library, the design's own breaking choice (section 10), entry `phonux-retire-max-panels-members`: four `SettingsAPI` members and `PanelsInput.storedMaxPanels` go (`settingsColumn` stays), `effectiveMaxPanels` loses its stored argument, the pack smoke loses its `storedMaxPanels` line. The app retires the setting in two entries: `app-retire-max-panels-on-screen` first, on 0.1.0 (whose `SettingsAPI` and `PanelsInput` still require the four members and `storedMaxPanels`, so the app passes inert values), then `app-adopt-phonux-0.2-imports` removes the inert values. Rev 6's window-width wrapper and owner question 1 are gone.
- Q18: no scrollbar; `tuning.rowGesture` stays 'step'; a step now scrolls by panel where 0.1.0 swapped a parked panel in, and a hidden panel still comes back through `revealOnScroll` (6B); a later custom scrollbar has its seam named and is deferred (3.7).
- Q19: per-panel `scrollOnDrag` and `tuning.edgeScroll`; entry `phonux-edge-scroll-while-dragging` before the release candidate; bounded by the strip, the box lands on its slot, mouse and pen only (6B). 61 descriptors, eleven tuning keys.
- Q20: the app ships `tuning.initialScroll: 'end'` (the newest closable panels in view at launch; pinned panels and History start off screen to the left); the library default stays 'start' (section 11). Q21: `tuning.edgeScroll` 48/900/500, `scrollOnOpen` 'into-view' and `scrollOnMove` 'follow' are owner-confirmed defaults (3.1).
- Rebase to phonux-lib main 7a60e82 (PR #4 hid ten values and three types from the barrel) and plan audit: citations moved; 0.2.0 is a BREAKING minor (section 10), 43 barrel values before this release's 19 additions (62); the app retargets its imports of the hidden names; the row-numbers entry is split from the max-panels retirement and the app adoption from the app's retirement, and the adoption is itself two issues (`app-adopt-phonux-0.2-imports`, then `app-adopt-phonux-0.2-workspace`; section 12); the root-less `marker` has evidence at every reader; the attach entry owns the template adapter; app entries satisfy the file-size ratchet and the phone-boundary test. No owner question remains: the launch position is Q20 and the scrolling defaults are Q21. Revision 7 also carries the third plan audit: the types of section 6 are declared in full, every entry that exports a type or value touches index.ts and the barrel test, and the barrel test's title and count move with the entry that adds the values (section 12).

## 0. Owner decisions in force
1-6 as in revision 4 (AppState and History are phonux-app's; `toggleVisibility()` is a History click on a hidden panel; a closable panel the person closed stays closed; every template has a unique id; every behaviour is a setting with a library default, no policy, no events; keepMounted, the look constants, reset and configure ship now).
7. Q13: eight tuning keys as specified. The design derived three more that the owner did not request: `rowGesture` and `initialScroll` (from Q15, workspace-only) and `edgeScroll` (from Q19, per panel): eleven keys, 61 descriptors, 23 entries. 8. Q1 holds under the defaults: Show, a History click and the row gesture reach a hidden panel. 9. Q11: three configure calls, a wrong-scope key is refused; Q12: `pinning` and `pinningWhenNotLive`; Q14: header buttons are generic slots (6A).
10. Q15, Q16: the row scrolls, a fixed panel may scroll off screen (Q15), and nothing is parked for room unless a panel's `overflow` says 'park' (Q16, the owner's words; 6B).
11. Q17: the owner retired the app's setting; dropping the library members that carried it is the design's own choice (section 10). 12. Q18: stepping stays, no scrollbar; a custom scrollbar is deferred (6B). 13. Q19: a dragged panel held near the window's edge scrolls the row, a per-panel setting (6B).
14. Q20: what a person sees when the app opens is the right end, newest first: phonux-app sets `tuning.initialScroll: 'end'`, so the newest closable panels are in view and the pinned panels and History start off screen to the left; the library default stays 'start' (section 11).
15. Q21: the scrolling defaults `tuning.edgeScroll` 48 px / 900 px per s / 500 ms, `scrollOnOpen` 'into-view' and `scrollOnMove` 'follow' are owner-confirmed; `revealOnScroll` 'edges' and the library's `tuning.initialScroll` 'start' remain design defaults the owner did not rule on (3.1).

## 1. Goals and non-goals
- G1. `createWorkspace()` returns a React-free object; `addPanel()` works before or after React mounts and returns a handle that stays valid.
- G2. Every engine behaviour that can differ between panels is a named setting (61 descriptors; section 4) with a documented default, one pure resolver (3.3), changeable after add (7.6); 3.7 lists the few fixed rules.
- G3. A PanelManager is the only gateway for per-panel change; each operation is checked against resolved settings and state, then refused with a typed code or made a documented no-op, never corrupting the row.
- G4. A read-only snapshot with stable ids (no events). G5. A breaking minor over 0.1.0 (section 10); testable without a real browser or host app except 8A's, 6B's and edge scrolling's rendering claims and real pixels (the app real-layout gate).

Dropped: History, AppState, multiple workspaces, a flush API, events, `PanelGroup`, `custom` header render items, a `<Workspace workspace>` shell, rename aliases, live `params` update, `layoutEpoch`, built-in pin/size/close controls, the shield (`evictable`), `no-room`, `stickyEdge` (3.7), rev 6's window-width wrapper (Q17).

## 2. What the engine does today (by reading)
Numbering is kept for cross-references; each owning entry pins its claims before it changes them.
- F3. `clearAll` keeps only `!collapsible` (useProvidePanels.ts:214-218): a locked parkable panel is wiped.
- F4. A park is not a hide: the commit scan stacks any unrecorded park (usePanelManager.ts:370-371), and a capacity rise (:389-404) or reveal-after-close (:409-431) returns it. The stack is unsaved.
- F7. Close and park paths call `flushSync` first (usePanelClosing.ts:113; usePanelManager.ts:160-167, :208-210, :259, :323); from a lifecycle it only logs.
- F8. Two closes before a re-render undo in the wrong order (usePanelClosing.ts:42-46, :117); `undoClose` reports every batch log id and a second call in one handler reads the stale `closed` (:131-133, :139, :164).
- F11. A right-side tie goes to the earlier array index, a left-side tie to the later (panelRowOrder.ts:77-79); only priority -1 lands nearest (:37).
- F12. An anchor naming an absent panel falls back to the root's right (panelRowOrder.ts:57-60, :64); hydrate maps every target the file lacks to one such id (workspaceState.ts:127-134).
- F14. A park flips `live`; the entry leaves the present filter (PanelRow.tsx:91), the exit plays, then the view unmounts (:127-154). `useViewState` cells outlive the unmount (viewState.ts:86-89, :137-147).
- F16. `DragToClose` closes after its exit; its `onClose` is required (dragToClose.tsx:175-181, :68). `movePanel` hops past the next PRESENT neighbour and splices array slots, so a parked panel between them shifts too (panelWindow.ts:249-261). A restore's success handler is the first argument of `.then(ok, fail)`, so a throw in it reaches neither (useWorkspacePersistence.ts:38-65).
- F17. The engine draws no title-bar button. `Panel` draws a header pill only when a view passes `title` (Panel.tsx:79; placeholders PanelFrame.tsx:188-204, :217-218) and a grab bar only with `grabProps` (not a tab stop: Panel.tsx:80-100). The app draws its title-bar controls as MUI `IconButton size="small"` with `sx={{ color: 'common.white' }}`: 'Panel size' (app: LivePanel.tsx:24-36, only on a closable panel) and 'Pin panel' with `aria-pressed` (:50-61). The library already depends on MUI (PanelFrame.tsx:9-11).
- F18. Pin today is a re-anchor: `panels.reanchor(id, { position: 'left' | 'right' })` (app: LivePanel.tsx:56); pinned is read from the anchor alone (`usePanelPinned`, PanelRow.tsx:53, :133); `reanchorPanel` ignores a non-collapsible entry (panelWindow.ts:97). Every drag re-anchors EVERY entry to the root (panelRowOrder.ts:97-100); with no root entry every entry reads 'right' (:24-32).
- F19. The capacity floor is fixed columns plus one (panelCapacity.ts:16-19); `capacityFor` floors live parkable panels at 1 (usePanelManager.ts:119). The raw `PanelsAPI` mutators take no gate (useProvidePanels.ts:177-208).
- F20. The row never scrolls: `Workspace` fixes `overflowX: 'hidden'` (Workspace.tsx:38-39, :57, :104-105; app: test/row-scroll-by-hiding.test.ts:1-7, 32 tests). Width is capped by capacity (panelRowLayout.ts:66-73) and the centring margin comes from `computeRowLayout` over a live set capped at what fits (Workspace.tsx:47-55), so an overflowing strip would get a blank left margin.
- F21. 'Row scroll' today is a swap, not a scroll. A horizontal swipe (every horizontal wheel event calls preventDefault and re-arms the 350 ms lock, only the first acts: useRowScrollGesture.ts:16, :33-46) or ArrowLeft/ArrowRight (no lock; skipping INPUT, TEXTAREA, SELECT and contentEditable targets, :47-54, listener on `window`, :56) calls `scrollRow` (usePanelManager.ts:305-327): it brings the first parked panel beyond the live range (panelWindow.ts:189-198) and parks the far-end live panel(s); with nothing parked on that side it does nothing. A fixed column is never reached or parked (panelWindow.ts:147, :175).
- F22. A new panel is always on screen and nothing scrolls to anything. `create` appends at its anchor and in the same update parks the farthest live panel(s) until the count fits (usePanelManager.ts:227-270); `activate` on a live panel does nothing (:278) and on a parked one flips it live and parks the farthest (:276-302; panelWindow.ts:208-223). A park exits to `x: -ownSpan`, an absolute strip coordinate (PanelRowSlot.tsx:62).

## 3. The settings model

### 3.1 Principle
phonux defines the complete catalog (section 4) as exported data, validates every layer in one resolver, enforces each setting at the engine's pure guard and the PanelManager, and documents each default. It picks no policy: each default reproduces 0.1.0 or an owner answer, except two design defaults the owner did not rule on, each a setting the owner may change: `revealOnScroll` 'edges' and the library's `tuning.initialScroll` 'start' (the app overrides it to 'end', Q20). The scrolling defaults `tuning.edgeScroll` (48 px, 900 px/s, 500 ms), `scrollOnOpen` 'into-view' and `scrollOnMove` 'follow' are owner-confirmed (Q21). The one deliberate departure from 0.1.0 is Q16, the owner's decision: every panel built through the cascade (a template, workspace defaults, a Host-bag create or a restore inside a workspace) defaults to `overflow: 'scroll'`; only a hand-built 0.1.0 entry in a row with no workspace defaults keeps 0.1.0's parking (3.6, 10).

### 3.2 The cascade
Last defined wins, field by field; `undefined` never overrides: 1 `LIBRARY_PANEL_DEFAULTS`; 2 `WorkspaceOptions.defaults` (a `tuning` key there is `invalid-settings`); 3 `template.settings`; 4 instance, two stored sub-layers: host (what a view asked at a Host-bag `create`, 3.5), then instance (`addPanel(t, { settings })` and every later `configure`, which wins; `null` deletes a value); 5 the person's overrides (anchor, tier, the pin's `parkable` half; only when `rememberLayout` is true). `placeOnAdd`, `placeOnShow`, `placeOnReturn`, `undoPlacement`, `formFactors`, `pinning` and `pinningWhenNotLive` are replaced whole; `tuning` merges per leaf; `headerButtons` merges per slot id (a later layer's slot replaces that id whole; `false` removes it). Derived defaults fill an unset field from another resolved field; forced rules override a field whatever a layer asked (3.4). `source` names the winner per key.

### 3.3 One resolver
`resolvePanelSettings(layers, { root, lenient })` is pure and the only code that interprets a layer: (1) validate each layer (unknown key, wrong type or member, out-of-range value, 'in-place' in `placeOnAdd`, an unreadable `pinning` (6A), an invalid `headerButtons` slot: `PanelManagerError('invalid-settings')` naming layer and key; `lenient`, used by restore for the file's own instance and user layers only, drops an invalid key with a note and never throws); (2) merge, the user layer's `parkable` as the top layer; (3) derived defaults; (4) forced rules C1-C6 in order; (5) the user layer's anchor and tier, checked against the resolved permissions; (6) notes. A conflict is recorded only when a template, instance or host layer asked for a value a forced rule overrides; a library or workspace collision is overridden silently (source 'forced'). `definePanel` runs it on the template layer alone; `addPanel`, restore, `configure` and the Host-bag create run all layers; `mergePatch(layer, patch)` is the one pure step behind `configure`. Notes go to `WorkspaceOptions.conflicts` ('warn' default, 'throw', 'off') and `PanelsInput.onNote`, once per panel and setting; runtime notes never throw.

### 3.4 Coupling rules
| id | when | forces |
|---|---|---|
| C1 | parkable:false (a fixed column: never hidden or parked, priced at phoneWidth, always in the strip, may scroll out of view) | resizable:false, formFactors:['phone'], formFactor:'phone', overflow:'scroll', startVisibility:'live', restoreVisibility:'live', keepMounted:false |
| C2 | closable:false | clearable:false |
| C3 | persist:'session' | rememberLayout:false, afterClose:'return-next-launch' |
| C4 | formFactor not in formFactors | formFactor = first allowed |
| C5 | the root | parkable:false (C1), closable:false (C2), draggable:false, placeable:false |
| C6 | viewState:'reset-on-park' | keepMounted:false |

Derived (any layer may override): draggable, placeable, listed follow `parkable`; clearable follows `parkable && closable`. Notes (never errors): `listed:false` with a held hide is showable by code only; a pinned half naming `parkable:false` while `listed` is unset is `pin-unlists`; `park-lever-inert` when a layer sets `capacityReturn`, `returnPriority`, `evictionRank` or `evictionOrder` on a panel whose resolved `overflow` is 'scroll'.

### 3.5 Who may set what
Template-scope settings (`hostCreatable`, `multiple`, `maxInstances`) live in the library, workspace-defaults and template layers; at the instance or host layer they are a `wrong-scope` conflict and ignored (`configure` refuses). Workspace-only tuning keys live in the library and `WorkspaceOptions.tuning` only: at the template layer they throw `invalid-settings`, at the instance or host layer they are a `wrong-scope` conflict. A view's door is the Host-bag `create`: it may set only `title`, `listed`, `layer`, `formFactor`, `placeOnAdd: { anchor }`, `enterFrom: 'anchor'`, never `keepMounted`, `headerButtons`, `overflow` or `pinning`. A template view receives `handle` (a `ViewHandle`: the panel's own verbs; its `configure` takes only those keys, anything else is `not-view-settable`) and `manager` (a `PanelReader`: read-only `get`, `list`, `getSnapshot`, `subscribe`), so a view can never make an unclosable panel or wipe the person's arrangement. App code that closes over a full handle is the app's own choice.

### 3.6 Where a resolved value lives
`PanelWindow.settings?: ResolvedPanelSettings` (frozen) and `key?` (permission entry), `instance?`, `overrides?`, `applied?`, `declared?` (persistence entry), `deferredPin?` (pin entry). One function, `applyTemplate(entry, resolved, mode)`, writes them: `create` writes everything (`locked = !closable`, `collapsible = parkable`, `listed`, `layer`, `anchoredEnter`, `formFactor`, `size`, `title`, `settings`, `instance`, `key`); `reconfigure` writes only `locked`, `listed`, `layer`, `anchoredEnter`, `title`, `settings`, `instance`, `deferredPin` and, for the parkable flip alone, `collapsible` (plus `live:true` when the flip makes the panel fixed), never `formFactor`, `size`, `anchor`, `hostData`, `key`, `overrides` or `applied`. `key` comes from `applyTemplate('create')` only: a hand-built entry has none, readers use `p.key ?? p.id`, and `appendPanel`'s output is unchanged (the 0.1.0 corpus holds). Engine code reads one accessor `effective(p)`: `p.settings`, else defaults derived from the legacy fields (`overflow: 'park'` for a collapsible entry, 'scroll' for a fixed one), so a hand-built 0.1.0 entry behaves as before. An entry carries `settings` whenever the row has workspace defaults (`PanelsInput.workspaceDefaults`, added by the overflow entry, always passed by `createWorkspace`; a Host-bag create of a plain `registerView` view resolves with it and no template layer; tests may seed `initialEntries` with `settings`); a row without them builds legacy entries. `ProvidedPanels.rowScroll` is true when any present entry carries `settings` whose `overflow` is 'scroll'; only then does the container scroll (6B).

### 3.7 What is not a panel setting (checked against the principle)
These stay fixed this release, each for a reason a non-engineer can check; the owner may promote any:
- Closing never moves the other panels: the row has no holes (panelWindow.ts:237). One shared undo window (usePanelClosing.ts:121-128). A settled row has one root and unique ids.
- In park mode the panel just opened or shown is never the one parked by its own action; with no `park` panel left, the row overflows and scrolls (no last-resort drop, no refusal). A capacity park takes as FEW `park` panels as possible (usePanelManager.ts:455-486); settings choose which, never how many.
- Tier widths (panelTiers.ts:19-33) and the app's History row travel are sizes the views and the app own.
- No `stickyEdge`: a panel cannot be kept on screen while the row scrolls (Q15 lets a pinned panel scroll away; a sticky layer needs its own z-order and a scroll-synchronised shift nothing asked for); it can be added later without changing anything here.
- No scrollbar this release (Q18): the row steps (swipe, Shift + wheel, arrow keys, History); `tuning.rowGesture` is workspace-wide because one wheel listener serves one container.
- Deferred, not built: a custom scrollbar (Q18; 6B names its seam), `stickyEdge`, and the review of the remaining exports (Jojos-Creations/phonux#2, item by item with the owner).

### 3.8 Tuning (owner Q6, Q13)
`WorkspaceOptions.tuning` (and `PanelsInput.tuning`) is the workspace layer of eleven keys. Q13's eight: `rowGap`, `dragToClose`, `slide`, `hopFraction` (the last three also per panel), `saveQuietMs` (500 ms), `gestureLockMs` (350 ms), `returnTieBreak` and `dropTieBreak` (they order panels the per-panel levers `returnPriority`, `evictionRank`, `evictionOrder` leave equal; park-mode only). Three the design derived, not requested: `rowGesture` and `initialScroll` (from Q15, workspace-only) and `edgeScroll` (from Q19, per panel; 6B). Defaults live in one leaf module, `rowConstants.ts`; `GAP`, `DRAG_CLOSE` and `SLOT_TRANSITION` keep names, shapes and values; views read `usePanelReorderSteps()` and `usePanelTuning()`. Their shapes: `usePanelReorderSteps(): (offsetX: number) => number` (neighbours, row gap and hop fraction come from the slot context, so the app's `reorderStepsForOffset(offsetX, neighborWidths, GAP)` becomes `steps(offsetX)`); `usePanelTuning(): ResolvedPanelTuning & { readonly rowGap: number }`; `slideToTransition(slide: SlideTuning): { duration: number; ease: [number, number, number, number] }` (copies the ease; returns `SLOT_TRANSITION` itself when the slide equals the default by value). `ROW_GAP` in `rowConstants.ts` is tagged `@public` with `GAP`'s doc sentence, because `GAP` re-exports it and `check:api-report` must see `GAP` unchanged.

## 4. The catalog (61 descriptors)
The catalog is the complete list (Appendix A); names are a key or `tuning.<leaf>`. The resolver entry writes each row (name, values, default, `on_configure`: class word, then rule) into the library DESIGN.md between `<!-- catalog:start -->` and `<!-- catalog:end -->`, and a docs-drift test keeps it equal to the descriptors.

## 5. Placement: explicit values
No bare 'left' or 'right' means a placement. `*` = a `parkable:false` column, P a parkable panel pinned left of the fixed column H, N the new panel; the placement entry pins the literal arrays for the row `[P H* A B]`.

| value | lands |
|---|---|
| row-start | left of every other entry, fixed columns and root included: `[N P H* A B]` |
| row-end | right of every other entry: `[P H* A B N]` |
| parkable-start | immediately left of the first parkable panel (live or parked); none: row-end |
| parkable-end | immediately right of the last parkable panel; none: row-end |
| after-fixed | immediately right of the last parkable:false entry; none: row-start: `[P H* N A B]` |
| `{ after: 'H' }`, `{ before: 'A' }` | immediately right of H, immediately left of A: `[P H* N A B]` |
| order | after the nearest predecessor in `WorkspaceOptions.order()` that is in the workspace, else before the nearest successor, else placeFallback |
| `{ anchor }` | an anchor with its `panel` named |
| in-place | its slot (placeOnShow and placeOnReturn only) |

A missing or self-naming target uses `placeFallback`; every 'immediately next to' value emits priority -1 (F11); row-end equals 0.1.0's default `{ position: 'right' }` and row-start the app's pin. `placePanel` runs inside `handleActivatePanel` before `promoteToLive`. Placement never scrolls; the row scrolls after the commit by `scrollOnOpen` or `scrollOnMove` (6B).

## 6. The public API
```ts
type PanelId = string   // singleton: its template id; explicit instance: as given; anonymous: `${templateId}~${random}`; 'all' reserved
type PanelStatus = 'pending' | 'live' | 'parked' | 'closed'   // live = in the strip, in view or not; hidden = parked with PanelState.held
type AnchorRequest = { panel?: PanelId; position: 'left' | 'right'; priority?: number }
type AddPlacement = 'row-start' | 'row-end' | 'parkable-start' | 'parkable-end' | 'after-fixed' | 'order'
  | { before: PanelId } | { after: PanelId } | { anchor: AnchorRequest & { panel: PanelId } }
type Overflow = 'scroll' | 'park'
type ScrollAlign = 'into-view' | 'start' | 'center'
type ScrollOnOpen = ScrollAlign | 'none'
type ScrollOnMove = 'follow' | 'none'
type ScrollOnDrag = 'edge' | 'none'
type HeaderButtonContext = { state: PanelState; settings: ResolvedPanelSettings }
type HeaderButton = {
  icon: React.ReactNode | ((c: HeaderButtonContext) => React.ReactNode)
  label: string | ((c: HeaderButtonContext) => string)          // accessible name, never empty
  onClick: (handle: ViewHandle, c: HeaderButtonContext & { announce(text: string): void }) => void | Promise<unknown>
  side?: 'start' | 'end'; order?: number                         // default 'end', 0; ascending, ties by id
  pressed?: (c: HeaderButtonContext) => boolean
  description?: string | ((c: HeaderButtonContext) => string); tooltip?: string | ((c: HeaderButtonContext) => string)
  disabled?: boolean | ((c: HeaderButtonContext) => boolean); hidden?: boolean | ((c: HeaderButtonContext) => boolean)
  sx?: SxProps<Theme>                                            // @mui/material
}
type PinHalf = { placement?: AddPlacement; parkable?: boolean }
type Pinning = { pinned: PinHalf; unpinned: PinHalf }
type PinWhenNotLive = 'place-only' | 'refuse' | 'show' | 'apply-on-next-show'
type PinningWhenNotLive = PinWhenNotLive | { parked?: PinWhenNotLive; hidden?: PinWhenNotLive; closed?: 'refuse' | 'apply-on-next-show' }
type SourceLayer = 'library' | 'workspace' | 'template' | 'host' | 'instance' | 'user' | 'derived' | 'forced'
// cascade order, lowest to highest: library < workspace < template < host < instance < user; 'derived' marks a value filled from
// another resolved field, 'forced' one overridden by a rule C1-C6 (the last step, so it wins over every layer)
type SettingValues = {
  closable: boolean; parkable: boolean; draggable: boolean; crossable: boolean; placeable: boolean; resizable: boolean
  formFactors: readonly FormFactor[]                       // non-empty
  clearable: boolean; hostCreatable: boolean; multiple: boolean
  maxInstances: number | 'unlimited'                       // integer >= 1
  listed: boolean
  startVisibility: 'live' | 'parked'; hideStickiness: 'sticky' | 'until-room'; hideWhenLast: 'allow' | 'refuse'
  capacityReturn: 'auto' | 'manual'; returnPriority: number; overflow: Overflow; evictionRank: number
  evictionOrder: 'farthest' | 'nearest' | 'leftmost' | 'rightmost'
  revealOnScroll: 'edges' | 'any' | 'never'; scrollOnOpen: ScrollOnOpen; scrollOnMove: ScrollOnMove; scrollOnDrag: ScrollOnDrag
  toggleWhenClosed: 'ignore' | 'reopen'; showWhenClosed: 'refuse' | 'reopen'
  viewState: 'keep' | 'reset-on-park'; keepMounted: boolean
  placeOnAdd: AddPlacement; placeOnShow: AddPlacement | 'in-place'; placeOnReturn: AddPlacement | 'in-place'
  placeFallback: 'row-start' | 'row-end' | 'parkable-start' | 'parkable-end' | 'after-fixed'
  undoPlacement: 'old-slot' | AddPlacement
  layer: 'base' | 'raised'; enterFrom: 'auto' | 'left' | 'right' | 'anchor'; exitTo: 'auto' | 'left' | 'right'
  closeExit: 'down' | 'left' | 'right'; animated: boolean
  formFactor: FormFactor; title: string
  pinning: Pinning; pinningWhenNotLive: PinningWhenNotLive
  persist: 'saved' | 'session'; rememberLayout: boolean; restoreVisibility: 'saved' | 'live' | 'parked'
  afterClose: 'stay-closed' | 'return-next-launch'
  undoWindowMs: number; reportClose: boolean; snapshot: 'both' | 'park' | 'close' | 'never'
}   // 49 keys: the catalog's 61 names less headerButtons and the 11 tuning leaves
type DragToCloseTuning = { distanceFraction: number; minDistancePx: number; flickMinDistancePx: number; flickVelocity: number; cancelVelocity: number; directionLockPx: number; snapBack: { stiffness: number; damping: number } }
type SlideTuning = { durationSec: number; ease: readonly [number, number, number, number]; closeExitSeconds: number }   // ease x1 and x2 in [0, 1]
type EdgeScrollTuning = { zonePx: number; maxPxPerSec: number; reducedStepMs: number }
type PanelTuning = {   // the per-panel leaves; each may also be set on the workspace
  dragToClose?: { [K in keyof DragToCloseTuning]?: DragToCloseTuning[K] }; slide?: { [K in keyof SlideTuning]?: SlideTuning[K] }
  hopFraction?: number; edgeScroll?: { [K in keyof EdgeScrollTuning]?: EdgeScrollTuning[K] }
}
type WorkspaceTuning = PanelTuning & {   // plus the seven workspace-only leaves
  rowGap?: number; saveQuietMs?: number; gestureLockMs?: number; returnTieBreak?: 'latest-first' | 'earliest-first' | 'row-order'
  dropTieBreak?: 'leftmost' | 'rightmost'; rowGesture?: 'step' | 'free' | 'off'; initialScroll?: 'start' | 'end' | 'root' | { panel: PanelId }
}
type ResolvedPanelTuning = { dragToClose: DragToCloseTuning; slide: SlideTuning; hopFraction: number; edgeScroll: EdgeScrollTuning }
type ResolvedWorkspaceTuning = ResolvedPanelTuning & { rowGap: number; saveQuietMs: number; gestureLockMs: number; returnTieBreak: 'latest-first' | 'earliest-first' | 'row-order'
  dropTieBreak: 'leftmost' | 'rightmost'; rowGesture: 'step' | 'free' | 'off'; initialScroll: 'start' | 'end' | 'root' | { panel: PanelId } }
type ResolvedPanelSettings = {   // deeply frozen; every key present after a resolve except title
  readonly [K in Exclude<keyof SettingValues, 'title'>]: SettingValues[K]
} & { readonly title?: string; readonly tuning: ResolvedPanelTuning; readonly headerButtons: { readonly [id: string]: HeaderButton } }
type ViewPatch = {   // exactly the keys a view may set on its own panel; any other key is not-view-settable; null deletes the instance value
  title?: string | null; listed?: boolean | null; layer?: 'base' | 'raised' | null; formFactor?: FormFactor | null
  placeOnAdd?: { anchor: AnchorRequest & { panel: PanelId } } | null; enterFrom?: 'anchor' | null
}
type SettingConflict = {   // recorded only for a template, host or instance layer that asked for what a rule or a scope forbids
  readonly key: string                                        // the catalog name that was asked (a tuning leaf is dotted: 'tuning.rowGap')
  readonly layer: 'template' | 'host' | 'instance'            // the SourceLayer that asked
  readonly kind: 'forced' | 'wrong-scope' | 'not-view-settable'
  readonly asked: unknown; readonly applied: unknown          // the asked value; the value the resolved settings hold instead
  readonly rule?: 'C1' | 'C2' | 'C3' | 'C4' | 'C5' | 'C6'    // present when kind is 'forced'
  readonly forcedBy?: string                                  // the catalog name whose value triggered the rule (C1 'parkable', C2 'closable', C3 'persist', C4 'formFactors', C6 'viewState'); absent for C5
  readonly message: string
}
type DeferredPin = { by: 'pin' | 'unpin' } | { parkable: boolean }
type WorkspaceStatus = 'detached' | 'loading' | 'ready' | 'disposed'
type ListFilter = { status?: PanelStatus | readonly PanelStatus[]; listed?: boolean }
interface PanelState {   // readonly snapshot of one panel
  readonly id: PanelId; readonly templateId: string; readonly status: PanelStatus; readonly held: boolean
  readonly engineId: string | null; readonly index: number | null      // null while the panel has no engine entry (pending or closed)
  readonly title: string | undefined; readonly url: string; readonly params: JsonValue | undefined
  readonly formFactor: FormFactor; readonly listed: boolean; readonly settings: ResolvedPanelSettings
  readonly pinned: boolean; readonly deferredPin: DeferredPin | null
}
type PanelSnapshot = { readonly version: number; readonly status: WorkspaceStatus; readonly panels: readonly PanelState[] }
type PanelReadHandle = {   // the five read-only members every handle and view handle has
  readonly id: PanelId; readonly templateId: string; getState(): PanelState
  getSettings(): ReturnType<typeof resolvePanelSettings>; subscribe(l: () => void): () => void
}
type PanelSettings = Partial<SettingValues> & { tuning?: PanelTuning; headerButtons?: { [id: string]: HeaderButton | false } }
type SettingsPatch = { [K in keyof PanelSettings]?: PanelSettings[K] | null }   // null deletes the instance-layer value
type InstancePatch = Omit<SettingsPatch, 'hostCreatable' | 'multiple' | 'maxInstances'>
type SettingNote = { code: string; panelId?: PanelId; key?: string; message: string }
declare const PANEL_SETTINGS_CATALOG: readonly SettingDescriptor[]   // name, group, values, default, scope, viewSettable, exists, ownerEntry, configure { cls: NOW | NEXT | STATE | SCOPE, doors }
declare function resolvePanelSettings(layers: { workspace?, template?, instance?, host?: PanelSettings; user?: { formFactor?: FormFactor; anchor?: AnchorRequest; parkable?: boolean } }, o?: { root?: boolean; lenient?: boolean }):
  { value: ResolvedPanelSettings; source: Record<string, SourceLayer>; conflicts: SettingConflict[]; notes: SettingNote[]; userAnchor?: AnchorRequest }

interface PanelTemplate<P extends JsonValue = JsonValue> {
  readonly id: string      // 1-48 chars, no whitespace, no '~', no leading '_', not all digits, not 'all'; unique in a workspace; the saved `view`
  readonly view: React.ComponentType<{ panel: PanelSummary<P>; handle: ViewHandle<P>; manager: PanelReader }>
  readonly settings?: PanelSettings; readonly params?: P
}
declare function definePanel<P extends JsonValue = JsonValue>(t: PanelTemplate<P>): PanelTemplate<P>   // identity plus validation
interface WorkspaceOptions {
  storage?: WorkspaceStorage; defaults?: Omit<PanelSettings, 'tuning'>; root?: PanelTemplate
  templates?: readonly PanelTemplate[]; order?: () => readonly PanelId[]; tuning?: WorkspaceTuning
  conflicts?: 'warn' | 'throw' | 'off'; newId?: () => string; onError?: (e: unknown) => void
}
declare function createWorkspace(o?: WorkspaceOptions): WorkspaceHandle
interface WorkspaceHandle {
  readonly rootId: string; readonly panels: PanelManager; readonly tuning: ResolvedWorkspaceTuning
  readonly status: WorkspaceStatus
  addPanel<P extends JsonValue>(t: PanelTemplate<P> | string, o?: { id?: PanelId; settings?: PanelSettings; params?: P; url?: string }): PanelHandle<P>
  registerTemplate(t: PanelTemplate): () => void
  dispose(): void
}
// PanelState.pinned is derived by the pure isPinned (6A); deferredPin is stored by pin() under 'apply-on-next-show'
type PanelRefusal = 'disposed' | 'not-attached' | 'not-ready' | 'is-pending' | 'is-closed' | 'not-live' | 'busy'
  | 'not-closable' | 'not-parkable' | 'not-draggable' | 'not-placeable' | 'not-resizable' | 'tier-not-allowed' | 'would-empty-row' | 'instance-limit'
  | 'unknown-panel' | 'wrong-scope' | 'would-reshape-row' | 'settings-conflict' | 'not-view-settable'
type PanelResult = { ok: true; changed: boolean } | { ok: false; code: PanelRefusal; message: string }   // changed:true = dispatched
type PinResult = PanelResult & { shown?: boolean; deferred?: boolean }
type ResetResult = PanelResult & { panels?: readonly PanelId[]; parked?: readonly PanelId[]; returned?: readonly PanelId[] }
type ConfigureResult = { ok: true; changed: boolean; shown: boolean; now: readonly string[]; later: readonly string[]; shadowed: readonly string[]; notes: readonly SettingNote[] }
  | { ok: false; code: PanelRefusal; message: string; setting?: string }
interface PanelHandle<P extends JsonValue = JsonValue> extends PanelReadHandle {
  getState(): PanelState & { readonly params: P | undefined }   // params typed by the template's P
  show(): PanelResult; hide(): PanelResult; toggleVisibility(): PanelResult; close(): PanelResult; open(): PanelResult
  move(direction: 'left' | 'right'): PanelResult; place(p: AddPlacement | 'in-place'): PanelResult; setFormFactor(next: FormFactor): PanelResult
  scrollTo(align?: ScrollAlign): PanelResult   // live: scroll the row to it (default: its scrollOnOpen, else 'into-view'); other states: 7.2
  pin(): PinResult; unpin(): PinResult
  configure(patch: InstancePatch): ConfigureResult
}
type ViewHandle<P extends JsonValue = JsonValue> = Omit<PanelHandle<P>, 'configure'> & { configure(patch: ViewPatch): ConfigureResult }
type PanelReader = { get(id: PanelId): PanelReadHandle | undefined; list(f?: ListFilter): readonly PanelState[]; getSnapshot(): PanelSnapshot; subscribe(l: () => void): () => void }
interface PanelManager {
  get<P extends JsonValue = JsonValue>(id: PanelId): PanelHandle<P> | undefined
  list(f?: ListFilter): readonly PanelHandle[]
  getSnapshot(): PanelSnapshot; subscribe(l: () => void): () => void
  configure(id: PanelId, patch: InstancePatch): ConfigureResult
  configureTemplate(templateId: string, patch: Pick<SettingsPatch, 'hostCreatable' | 'multiple' | 'maxInstances'>): ConfigureResult
  configureTuning(patch: { [K in keyof WorkspaceTuning]?: WorkspaceTuning[K] | null }): ConfigureResult
  resetLayout(target: PanelId | 'all'): ResetResult
}
```
`PanelManagerError` (programmer errors only): invalid-template, duplicate-template, unknown-template, template-collision, root-mismatch, invalid-options, invalid-settings, instance-id-required, already-attached, disposed. The additive React helpers, optional props, `ProvidedPanels` members and the type-export rule are in section 10. The declarations above (`SourceLayer` to `PanelReadHandle`) are the one source of the shapes: the resolver entry exports each as a type, and every other entry cites field names from them.

`addPanel`: a singleton is get-or-create by id; an explicit `id` is required for a `multiple` template before ready; over `maxInstances` it returns a handle in status 'closed' whose `open()` says `instance-limit`. A handle holds `(workspace, id)` and resolves the engine id on every call. Engine controls reach the manager through a module-private WeakMap.

## 6A. Pinning and header buttons (owner Q12, Q14)

**Pin (`pinning`).** Two halves, each with an optional `placement` (any `placeOnAdd` value, never a bare 'left') and an optional `parkable`. Default (F18): pinned = `row-start`, unpinned = `row-end`; a pinned panel is an ordinary panel left of the root and scrolls like any other. `{ pinned: { parkable: false }, unpinned: { parkable: true } }` makes a pin a fixed column (it may scroll off screen, Q15).

*Pinned is read, never stored, so a definition must stay readable after a drag* (a drag re-anchors every entry to the root). Valid only with a discriminator: (A) both halves name `parkable`, opposite values (read: resolved `parkable` equals the pinned half's); or (B) pinned placement `row-start` and unpinned `row-end` or `after-fixed` (read: left of the root). If both hold both are read. Any other placement may appear only beside (A): applied, never read. An empty definition is valid (reads false); anything else, or `parkable` in one half only, throws `invalid-settings`. A root-less workspace keeps a marker entry (8), so (B) reads there too. `usePanelPinned` keeps its 0.1.0 meaning (left of the root); `PanelState.pinned` follows the definition.

*Applying a half* (`pin()`, `unpin()`) is all or nothing in one commit: `pin()` places (gate `placeable`), then fixes; `unpin()` frees first (re-deriving `listed`, `draggable`, `placeable`, `clearable`), then places, so a fixed pin can always be unpinned. Fixing needs the phone tier (`tier-not-allowed`) and no conflicting explicit setting (`settings-conflict`). A fixed pin never needs room: it may scroll out of view (Q15), and in a row holding `park` panels the capacity reconcile parks the fewest of them (floor F19). A fixed pin unlists the panel (note `pin-unlists`). After an accepted pin or unpin `scrollOnMove` applies (6B). A header-button press is the person's gesture: verbs the click function calls synchronously record as a drag does (`overrides.anchor`, and `overrides.parkable` for the parkable half), saved under `rememberLayout` and cleared by `resetLayout`; a verb from code records nothing.

**A panel that is not live (`pinningWhenNotLive`).** One value, or one per status `{ parked, hidden, closed }`. K engine-parked, H hidden, C closed declared, U closed inside the undo window, P pending, A closed anonymous, F same turn:

| status | place-only (default) | refuse | show | apply-on-next-show |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| live | applies now | same | same | same |
| K, H | the placement half applies in place, the panel stays parked or held; a half naming `parkable` is refused whole, `would-reshape-row` | whole pin: `would-reshape-row` | the show path runs (hold released), then the change applies in the same commit; `shown:true` | stored as `deferredPin` |
| C, U | `is-closed` | `is-closed` | `is-closed` | stored on the record, applied by open() or undo |
| P, A, F | `is-pending`, `is-closed`, `busy` | same | same | same |

For `closed` only refuse and apply-on-next-show mean anything. *deferredPin* is `{ by: 'pin' | 'unpin' } | { parkable: boolean } | null` (session only): applied the next time the panel becomes live by ANY path, inside that path's updater (placement, room-making and enter side, then the `parkable` flip, which sets `live:true` when it makes the panel fixed); re-checked then and dropped with a runtime note if it fails; a call whose result equals the current read cancels it; cleared by a live pin, unpin or `configure(parkable)`, a close and `resetLayout`; `configure(pinning)` keeps it.

**Header buttons (owner Q14).** The developer declares generic slots in `headerButtons`, keyed by slot id (1-32 chars of a-z, 0-9, '-'), in a template, the workspace defaults or an instance. A slot is a `HeaderButton` (section 6): `icon`, accessible `label`, `onClick`, and optionally `side`, `order`, `pressed`, `description`, `tooltip`, `disabled`, `hidden`, `sx`. The library ships no built-in button; nothing is drawn unless a layer declares one. The catalog row `headerButtons` and the header-button entry hold the details; the contract:
- *Drawn* by `Panel` (Panel.tsx:79) from the slot context PanelRow provides (PanelRow.tsx:131-134), as MUI `IconButton`s (`size="small"`, native buttons): end-side slots after the view's own actions in `PanelHeaderRow`'s `actions`, ordered by `order` then id; start-side slots in a new `leading` prop before the title; with nothing to draw `Panel` passes `undefined`, so the dimmed 'more' placeholder stays (PanelFrame.tsx:196-204, :214, :218). `sx` defaults to `{ color: 'inherit' }` and a slot's own `sx` replaces it whole, so the app passes `{ color: 'common.white' }` and its markup equals today's (F17). A `Panel` with no `title` has no header, so no slots.
- *Focus.* Tab order is DOM order (start slots, title, the view's actions, end slots; the grab bar is no tab stop); Enter and Space press; the label is the accessible name, `pressed` is `aria-pressed`. After a press focus stays on the button even when the panel moves (`focus({ preventScroll: true })` in a layout effect: a plain `focus()` would jump the row natively and fight 6B); if the panel leaves the row or the button disappears, focus goes to the header row (tabindex -1, present only while a slot is declared) of the panel that takes the slot, else the previous one.
- *Click and safety.* `onClick(handle, ctx)` receives the panel's own `ViewHandle` (its verbs only) and runs through the in-flight gate; verbs called synchronously are the person's gesture, after an `await` they are code; a refused verb returns its typed code to the function, which decides what to show (`ctx.announce(text)`). Each slot has its own error boundary (a throw while rendering, or an empty `label`, hides that slot with a runtime note); an `onClick` error goes to `WorkspaceOptions.onError`, else `console.error`; a `disabled` slot stays focusable with `aria-disabled` and does nothing on press; a slot of an exiting panel is inert.
- *Cascade, configure, own header.* Layers merge per slot id; `false` removes a slot; `null` in a patch deletes the instance entry; a view cannot declare slots (`not-view-settable`). `configure({ headerButtons })` is NOW; a parked or hidden panel stores it. `<Panel headerButtons={false}>` suppresses the cluster; `usePanelHeaderButtons()` returns the resolved slots (with `press()`) and `<PanelHeaderButtons />` renders the cluster inside a custom header; outside a slot both return nothing.

## 6B. Overflow, scrolling and parking (owner Q15, Q16, Q18, Q19)

*What the answers mean (F20-F22).* The row now scrolls horizontally and every open panel stays in it, so nothing is parked for room unless a panel's `overflow` says 'park' (Q16); a pinned or fixed panel, and History, may scroll off screen (Q15); opening a panel scrolls the row to it. Terms: *open*: in the row (live or hidden); *live*: in the strip, in view or not; *in view*: live and overlapping the window (the scroll container's visible width less its padding); *hidden*: not in the strip: parked by `hide()` or, only for a panel whose `overflow` is 'park', by the engine for lack of room; listed in History.

*Settings* (rows in Appendix A). `overflow` ('scroll' default | 'park', per panel) says what the row may do with THIS panel when the open panels do not all fit: 'scroll' (Q16) never parks it for room, so it keeps its slot and may scroll out of view; 'park' lets the engine park it (0.1.0's behaviour), fewest first, never the panel the action just opened or showed, and return it when room opens. `park` is for a memory limit and 0.1.0 compatibility (no person-set count exists, Q17); `scroll` panels are the overflow, `park` panels only reduce it, and what remains scrolls; a fixed column forces 'scroll' (C1). `scrollOnOpen`, `scrollOnMove` and `revealOnScroll` are per panel; `tuning.rowGesture` and `tuning.initialScroll` are workspace-only (the rows hold their values and timing).

*The row's numbers.* One pure `computeRowNumbers({ entries, phoneWidth, containerWidth, gap, fixedItemCount? })` is read by `useProvidePanels`, `usePanelManager`, `Workspace` and `PanelRow`, so they cannot disagree. It returns `rowScroll`; `windowWidth` (container less 2 gaps; unmeasured: Infinity); `stripWidth`; `rowMarginLeft`; `ids`, `widths` and `leftOffsets` of the present entries in row order; `maxLiveWidth`; `reservedWidths` (present non-`park` panels) and `parkLiveWidths` for park-mode capacity. `isPresent` keeps the root-less marker (section 8) and every hidden or parked entry out of all of them. A legacy row (no `settings`) gets today's numbers, margin included. In a scrolling row `rowMarginLeft` is 0 when the strip is wider than the window and otherwise (windowWidth - stripWidth)/2 over ALL present widths, uncapped, so an overflowing strip has no blank margin and a row that fits stays centred; `maxLiveWidth` is max(phoneWidth, windowWidth). A panel's left edge in scroll coordinates is gap + rowMarginLeft + leftOffsets[k], with the target margin of the same commit. The farthest legal scrollLeft is max(0, rowMarginLeft + stripWidth - windowWidth), computed from these numbers and never read back from the DOM. When `rowScroll` turns false, scrollLeft resets to 0. One pure function of `rowScroll` and `rowGesture` sets the container's overflow: a legacy row `hidden`; 'step' `overflow-x: auto` with the scrollbar hidden; 'free' `auto` with the native bar; 'off' `hidden` (code, History and edge scrolling still scroll).

*The scroller and the gesture.* One internal object per scroll container, `RowScroller`, is the only writer of its scrollLeft: `glideTo` (scrollOnOpen, scrollOnMove, `scrollTo`, History), `stop`, and `claim()`, an exclusive writer for one holder (`write`, `setMaxScroll`, `release`). Workspace marks the container `data-row-scroller`; the scroller is found from any descendant by that mark, never by computed overflow. A glide uses `tuning.slide` (instant under `animated:false` or reduced motion) and stops at once on pointer-down in the container or on a scroll it did not write (more than 1 px off its last value); the wheel and arrow events the step gesture itself consumes never cancel the glide they started. Q18 keeps the inputs; what a 'step' does changes because the row now scrolls. 'step': a horizontal swipe (one step per burst, `tuning.gestureLockMs`), Shift+wheel or ArrowLeft/ArrowRight (the 0.1.0 filters: not in INPUT, TEXTAREA, SELECT or contentEditable; no modifier) scrolls to the next panel start in that direction, where 0.1.0 swapped a parked panel in (F21), or reveals the hidden candidate above (Q1: the gesture still shows a hidden panel); at the strip's end with nothing hidden it does nothing. A step's base is the in-flight glide's target, so ten arrow repeats advance ten stops. 'free' and 'off' install no listener. While a claim is held `glideTo` is refused (the request is dropped with the note `scroll-dropped-during-drag`) and the gesture does nothing.

*Scrolling while a panel is dragged (Q19).* `scrollOnDrag` ('edge' default | 'none', per panel) and `tuning.edgeScroll` (per panel or workspace: `zonePx` 48, `maxPxPerSec` 900, `reducedStepMs` 500) let a horizontal-locked reorder drag in a scrolling row scroll it: a pointer in a band `zonePx` wide inside either visible edge of the container, or beyond it, scrolls the row that way at `maxPxPerSec` x depth (linear, 0 at the band's inner edge). Mouse and pen only: the grab handle is `touch-action: pan-x` (dragToClose.tsx:127), so a touch swipe is a native pan, never a reorder, and its `pointercancel` releases the claim with no scroll. It works under every `rowGesture`, 'off' included. The limit is the strip's, never the DOM's: the dragged box is a transformed descendant that CSS counts into scrollable overflow (dragToClose.tsx:85 locks overflow-y for the same reason), so `scrollWidth` would grow with the box and a DOM bound would never be reached; the claim gets `setMaxScroll` from the row numbers at drag start and on every commit, and the loop and the reduced-motion `stepTarget` use it. The scroll distance counts from scrollLeft at pointer-down. The box is drawn at Motion's drag x plus a second MotionValue (the scroll distance) rendered as the CSS `translate` property, which Motion's rewrites of `transform` cannot overwrite; `onDragEnd` reports pointer offset plus that distance, so the unchanged hop rule counts every crossed panel at release. At release both components animate to 0 together while the slot animates to its new rest (reduced motion: jump together), so the box lands on its slot and does not jump. A vertical-locked drag never scrolls and closes as today; a horizontal-locked one never closes. Reduced motion steps by panel starts per `reducedStepMs`. The drag holds the scroller's claim: programmatic scrolls are dropped with a note and the row gesture is inert; if another drag already holds it this one does not edge-scroll (reorder and close still work); a native scroll during the drag is added to under 'free' and cannot happen under 'step' or 'off'; a glide in flight stops at pointer-down (the two Appendix A rows hold the rest).

*A later custom scrollbar (Q18; deferred, not an entry).* Nothing here blocks it. It would read the strip from `computeRowNumbers` and the position from scrollLeft through the same `RowScroller`, scroll by `claim()` as edge scrolling does, and be switched by a new workspace tuning key beside `tuning.rowGesture` (working name `rowScrollbar`, default 'none'); 'free' already shows the native bar. `RowScroller` stays internal until then.

## 7. What 'safe' means

### 7.1 The contract
1. Every handle method validates against the resolved settings and committed state before the engine is touched; the only same-turn state it consults is the in-flight gate (7.4).
2. A refusal returns `{ ok: false, code }` and calls nothing: `entries` keeps its reference, no `setPanels` runs, no commit follows. A true no-op returns `{ ok: true, changed: false }`. Methods never throw for runtime state.
3. Each setting is enforced by the manager (typed refusal) and the engine's pure guard (same-array no-op) on every creation path: addPanel, restore, Host-bag create, late registration.

### 7.2 Outcomes (operation by state)
| op | live | parked | closed | pending |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| show | scrolls per scrollOnOpen if out of view; changed:true only when a scroll was dispatched | placeOnShow, activate (may park `park` panels), scroll | showWhenClosed: refuse = `is-closed`; reopen = open() | `is-pending` |
| hide | parkable else `not-parkable`; `hideWhenLast:'refuse'` with no other panel left once this turn's hides and closes count: `would-empty-row`; else park, hold per hideStickiness | hold per hideStickiness | `is-closed` | `is-pending` |
| toggle | hide | show | per toggleWhenClosed | `is-pending` |
| close | closable else `not-closable` | same | ok, unchanged | `is-pending` |
| open | ok, unchanged | ok, unchanged | declared: re-add at placeOnAdd and scroll per scrollOnOpen (`instance-limit` when full); anonymous: `is-closed` | `is-pending` |
| move | draggable else `not-draggable` | `not-live` | `is-closed` | `is-pending` |
| place | placeable else `not-placeable` | places, stays parked | `is-closed` | `is-pending` |
| setFormFactor | resizable else `not-resizable`; tier in formFactors else `tier-not-allowed` | same, stays parked | `is-closed` | `is-pending` |
| scrollTo | scrolls (`changed:false` if already there, or while a drag holds the row) | `not-live` | `is-closed` | `is-pending` |
| pin, unpin | applies the half (6A) | per pinningWhenNotLive | declared: apply-on-next-show stores on the record, else `is-closed` | `is-pending` |

An accepted move, place, setFormFactor, pin or unpin on a live panel then follows `scrollOnMove` (6B). `configure` and `resetLayout` follow 7.6 and 7.7. `disposed`, `not-attached` and `not-ready` refuse every imperative call; only `addPanel` queues.

### 7.3 Invariants a test can pin
- S0 resolver: pure; forced rules hold over 1000 random layer stacks; `conflicts` lists exactly what a template, instance or host layer asked otherwise.
- S1 row: ids and keys unique, order stable, one root; after every SETTLED commit (a window change, `tuning.rowGap` change or `parkable` flip settles in two) on a measured row the live `park` panels fit the window beside the non-`park` panels except the floor (F19). A refusal leaves `entries` reference-equal with no commit.
- S2 every setting holds on every creation path (restore alone may exceed `multiple` and `maxInstances`). S3 at least 500 random sequences over random settings vectors: no restricting setting is bypassed and show() always works.
- S5 two operations on one panel in one turn give `busy` for the second; a close is exclusive (7.4). S6 restore(save(x)) is stable over two launches; a changed default reaches untouched panels only; the person's overrides survive any configure; `deferredPin` is never saved.
- S7 every name has a doc row, enforcement site, owner entry, `on_configure` class and a differential test; a test that ran no scenario fails the build. S8 one probe per catalog row and a per-class status table; S9 reset lands declared panels in their literal default arrangement, a second call is `changed:false`; S10 `pin()` then `unpin()` restores row order, History listing and permissions for every valid definition, and a drag of an unrelated panel leaves the read unchanged.
- S11 scroll: with every panel 'scroll', over 500 random sequences no panel is ever parked for room; a row wider than the window has `computeRowNumbers` stripWidth above windowWidth and `rowMarginLeft` 0 (the real `scrollWidth` is the real-layout gate's: jsdom has no layout); after an open with `scrollOnOpen` 'into-view' the panel lies inside the window; `park` panels alone are ever parked for room.
- S12 edge scroll: with `scrollOnDrag` 'none' a held drag never changes scrollLeft; with 'edge' the released offset is the pointer offset plus the row's scroll distance, scrollLeft never exceeds the strip's bound, the box lands on its slot; the scroller's claim is held only while a drag is.

### 7.4 Concurrency
- Commits: a pointer to the newest committed engine controls is written in a dependency-free `useLayoutEffect`, never during render.
- In-flight gate: a ref counter `seq`, a map `inflight: PanelId -> { token, source: 'manager' | 'raw' }`, `closeToken` and a reducer `published`. An accepted manager call takes `token = ++seq`, calls the engine and dispatches `published = max(published, token)`; each commit's layout effect publishes the snapshot and deletes tokens at most `published`, and the dispatch forces a commit when the engine ignored the call. The raw `PanelsAPI` mutators close, undoClose, activate, move, reanchor and setFormFactor take a 'raw' token on the ids they touch and otherwise run as in 0.1.0 (they never refuse; a raw call on an id holding a manager token is a no-op); `clearAll` and `create` take no token and are never no-ops.
- `busy`: the id holds any token. A close is exclusive in its turn (F7, F8): it answers `busy` when any manager operation already ran in the turn, and any manager operation after a close answers `busy`. Operations on different panels otherwise combine, except that `would-empty-row` counts the turn's in-flight hides, and `resetLayout('all')` and `configureTuning({ rowGap })` are `busy` while any id holds a token. A scroll request takes no token and is never `busy`; one recorded while a drag holds the row is dropped (6B).
- Declarative queue: an `addPanel` before ready is an intent replayed at each settle; imperative calls before settle are `not-ready`. From a lifecycle, handle methods run but React skips the flushed exit mark; `configure` never flushes.

### 7.5 Lifecycle
Unmount detaches (`status` `detached`; handles answer `getState()`, imperative calls return `not-attached`). `dispose()` drops listeners and intents; a later `addPanel` throws `disposed`. Registering a template over a plain `registerView` value throws `template-collision`; `registerView` over a template id wins for rendering.

### 7.6 configure: changing a setting after add (owner Q8, Q11)
`handle.configure(patch)` or `manager.configure(id, patch)`; `null` removes an instance value. A configure from code is a developer instance override: it edits the instance layer, never the person's arrangement. Three calls: `configure(id, InstancePatch)`, `configureTemplate(templateId, ...)`, `configureTuning(patch)`; any other call with a known key answers `wrong-scope`, an unknown template or key throws. All or nothing. Status letters in each `on_configure`: L live; K parked (engine); H hidden (held); T acted on this turn; C closed declared past the undo window; U closed inside it; P pending; F same turn; A closed anonymous. **Unless a row says otherwise: P `is-pending`, F `busy`, A `is-closed`, C and U store the edit on the record (C: used by the next open(); U: by the pending undo).**
1. Gate, engine untouched: workspace status; `unknown-panel`; `busy`; P; A. C and U store the edit and answer `later`; `closed[]` is never edited.
2. Validate (unknown key, wrong type or out-of-range leaf throws `invalid-settings`; an empty or equal patch is `{ ok: true, changed: false }`), then re-resolve the whole stack: a conflict the current stack did not have refuses `settings-conflict` naming the key; a pre-existing one does not.
3. State check by class. NOW: a button, a History row, a z-index or the row's price changes at this commit; nothing else moves. NEXT: stored; the first event that reads it comes later; whatever is recorded (a hold, a stack entry, an open undo window, a saved anchor) keeps its rule: configure parks no live panel, shows no hidden one, moves none and scrolls nothing. STATE: `parkable` is the pin flip (live true->false needs the phone tier); `formFactors` excluding the current tier is `tier-not-allowed`; `keepMounted` and `viewState` act through the park latches (8A). SCOPE: `multiple` -> false, or `maxInstances` below the instances that exist, is `instance-limit`.
4. Commit: ONE `setPanels` updater rewrites this panel through `applyTemplate(reconfigure)`; every other entry stays reference-equal. No flushSync; one-shot side effects run in the commit's layout effect, never inside an updater.
5. Result: `now`, `later`, `shadowed` (outvoted by the person's tier or pin, or a forced value) and `shown`.

Undo: `usePanelClosing` takes `reapply(panel)` (identity without a workspace) and rebuilds the closed entry by `applyTemplate(reconfigure)` from the record's current layers, applying a stored `deferredPin`, at a `placeAs` (`reapply(panel): { panel, placeAs }`, where `placeAs` is the rebuilt panel's resolved `undoPlacement`: `'old-slot'` restores at the recorded index as 0.1.0 does, any other value is placed through `resolvePlacement`). The instance layer is saved (9) only for panels code does not re-declare (Q10); `configureTemplate` and `configureTuning` are session-only.

### 7.7 resetLayout (owner Q7)
`resetLayout(id | 'all')` clears the person's overrides and any deferred pin and applies the cascade defaults now, in one `setPanels` and one token. The named panels (all but the root) are re-placed by their resolved `placeOnAdd`, declared panels first in declaration order, and take the resolved `formFactor` and `parkable`; live panels move and resize in place, parked and hidden ones are re-placed and stay parked or held. One named panel follows its `scrollOnMove`; 'all' scrolls nothing. A tier that no longer fits parks the fewest `park` panels; the result lists `parked` and `returned`. C clears overrides so `open()` places by default, U marks the record reset, A is `is-closed` for one id and skipped by 'all', P is `is-pending`. Instance settings, holds and template defaults are untouched. Every named entry saves `overrides: {}` and `applied.placeOnAdd`; `changed:false` when nothing moved, resized or was cleared.

## 8. The root and the fixed column
The engine keeps `rootId` internally; the public workspace makes the root optional. With `root`, the template becomes the engine root (id = template id): C5, unlisted, no anchor, seeded into the first paint. With none, the reserved id `'__root__'` is a marker entry (`marker: true`) that stays in the array and is never present: `isPresent` (panelRowEntry.ts) is false for it, so PanelRow draws no slot, widths, leftOffsets and neighbour lists exclude it, `movePanel` never hops over it, `computeRowNumbers` skips it and `fixedViewCount` is 0; without it every entry would read 'right' (F18). A fixed column (`parkable:false`) is never hidden, parked, dragged or resized, always live and in the strip; Q15 says it may scroll off screen, so 'fixed' no longer means 'always visible' and no setting keeps it visible (3.7). The root-less engine and the marker are built and pinned in the placement entry, a hard gate; a defect it finds is fixed there, or `root` is typed required in 0.2.0.

## 8A. The parked-but-mounted path (`keepMounted`, owner Q6)
`keepMounted:true` keeps a parked panel's view mounted but not rendered (`display:none`, `inert`, `aria-hidden`; its state, DOM, canvas and media live on); `false` (default) is today's unmount after the park exit (F14). A per-entry latch decides at the park commit (the `viewState` latch is the same); geometry is computed over `present` alone, so kept entries change no number (6B). `usePanelHidden()` lets a view pause; no memory cap (Q9); never view-settable or saved. A live panel scrolled out of view is mounted and not hidden. jsdom proves structure only; the app real-layout gate proves rendering.

## 9. Persistence
One workspace, one `WorkspaceStorage` slot, `version` stays 1. Settings are never saved; saved are data, the developer's asks, the person's overrides and which default placed each panel. Per saved panel (additive keys, written only for entries that carry `settings`; a hand-built entry projects exactly as in 0.1.0): `key`; `instance` (only for entries code does not re-declare); `overrides: { anchor?, formFactor?, parkable? }` (present, possibly empty); `applied: { placeOnAdd }`; `closable?: false`. Top level: `dismissed?: string[]` (at most 256) and `root?`. Never saved: `hostData`, `size`, `locked`, `collapsible`, the root, the park stack, the held set, `deferredPin`, a kept-mounted view, tuning, the scroll position.

Who owns what (Q2): an entry without `overrides` is a 0.1.0 entry and owns its anchor and tier; with `overrides` it owns each key present. An untouched entry whose resolved `placeOnAdd` equals `applied.placeOnAdd` keeps its saved anchor; otherwise it is re-placed. Permission settings gate gestures, not restoring; only a tier outside `formFactors` is dropped, with a note. `rememberLayout:false` applies and records nothing but carries stored overrides through every save. Only PanelsAPI calls and the person's header-button presses record, at the hook layer and only for entries carrying `settings`; a drag marks every non-root remembering panel; code verbs, `configure`, `resetLayout` and the `placeOn*` settings never mark.

Restore reads through a sanitizer and resolves the file's own layers leniently; its success handler is wrapped, so a throw in reconcile takes the failed-load path. Reconcile is one pure function in `load().then`, in a single `setPanels`: draw every fresh id first; rebuild a saved entry of a registered template by the cascade, or hydrate an unregistered one as 0.1.0 does; create each declared panel with no saved entry unless its id is in `dismissed` (an `afterClose:'stay-closed'` panel that leaves the list enters it, and leaves it when it returns); let `restoreVisibility` decide each `live`; run `computeRowOrder` once. A `persist:'session'` panel is not written, and a saved anchor naming one is rewritten from the filtered row order (F12). First paint and restore never scroll by `scrollOnOpen`; `tuning.initialScroll` positions the strip once, after the restore has settled (6B).

## 10. Compatibility and versioning
- Ship as 0.2.0, first as `0.2.0-rc.N`. 0.2.0 is a BREAKING minor release (0.x semver). Main (7a60e82) already removed the 22 deprecated aliases, the two internal package keys and, after the export review, ten values and three types hidden from the barrel (CHANGELOG 'Unreleased / Removed'); the barrel test pins 43 runtime values before this release's 19 additions (62), added by seven entries (resolver +7 to 50, permissions +1 to 51, look tuning +3 to 54, keepMounted +1 to 55, row numbers +1 to 56, attach +4 to 60, header buttons +2 to 62); each of them edits the test's title ('exactly the N runtime values') and its list together in its own commit. Jojos-Creations/phonux#2 reviews the remaining exports with the owner item by item; this design removes only what it needs.
- What breaks: (1) the 22 aliases (gone) and the ten values and three types the export review hid from `phonux` (gone: OverlayProvider, OverlayStore, PanelRowSlot, shouldCloseOnRelease, computeRowOrder, deriveAnchorsFromOrder, summarisePanel, computeMaxLiveWidth, CaptureRegistryContext, vendorLabelForUsbVendorId; types Item, Entry, ReleaseMeasure). (2) For Q17, the design's own choice, which the owner may reverse: `SettingsAPI.maxPanelsOnScreen`, `fitCount`, `minPanelsOnScreen` and `setMaxPanelsOnScreen` are removed, as are the same members of `createFakeHost().settings`; `settingsColumn` stays. They are required members of an interface a Host implements, the app reads only `settingsColumn` once its setting is retired, and phonux picks no max-panels policy; making them optional is the alternative and leaves a dead API, for #2 to revisit. (3) `PanelsInput.storedMaxPanels`; `effectiveMaxPanels` loses its stored argument. (4) Behaviour, in CHANGELOG and pinned by a compat caller seeding `{ locked: true, collapsible: true }`: a panel built through a workspace scrolls instead of parking (Q16) and its container's overflow-x, centring margin and `maxLiveWidth` change; `clearAll` keeps locked entries (F3); `usePanelCloseEligible` becomes `collapsible && !locked`; `undoClose` consumes its batch once and reports only ids it restored (F8); a raw call on an id holding a manager token is a no-op; in a scrolling row `ReorderHandle.onDragEnd`'s offset includes the scroll distance. (5) `ProvidedPanels` gains members, so a hand-typed one stops compiling. (6) The @public `PanelsAPI` comments that promise parking at capacity (hostApi.tsx:138-139, :157, :166, :176-179, :192) are reworded: parking applies to panels whose `overflow` is 'park'. Not breaking: new optional fields, props and last parameters (`DragToClose.onClose` becomes optional); a 0.1.0 saved file restores unchanged and a file this build writes passes 0.1.0's `isWorkspaceState`.
- Defaults equal 0.1.0 for every hand-built 0.1.0 entry and call (`effective()` gives them `overflow:'park'`; a row with no `settings` entry keeps `overflowX:'hidden'`). `GAP`, `DRAG_CLOSE`, `SLOT_TRANSITION` and `PANEL_TRANSITION` keep names, shapes and values. New files raise `MIN_LIBRARY_FILES` (test/boundary.test.ts:17-21), are never named `useProvide*`, and pointer, reducer and gate code has no `useMemo` or `useCallback` (test/source-invariants.test.ts:50-80).
- rc first, published with `--tag next` (tools/phonux-release-check.mjs:43 prints a plain `npm publish`); the owner publishes, no agent does.
- Additive public surface. React and helpers, additive: `usePanelStates`, `usePanelState`, `useWorkspaceManager`, `usePanelCapabilities`, `usePanelHidden` (8A), `usePanelTuning`, `usePanelReorderSteps` (3.8), `usePanelHeaderButtons()` and `<PanelHeaderButtons />` (6A), `computeRowNumbers` (6B). Optional new props: `Panel` `headerButtons?: false`; `PanelHeaderRow` `leading`; `Workspace` `rowScroll`, `tuning`, `rowNumbers`; `PanelRow` `workspace` (the `WorkspaceHandle`, from which PanelRow builds each slot's `ViewHandle` and button context), `tuning`, `rowNumbers`; `PanelsInput` `workspace` (the `WorkspaceHandle`; with it `storage` and `initialEntries` are development errors), `workspaceDefaults`, `templates`, `declared`, `tuning`, `onNote`. `ProvidedPanels` gains members `rowScroll`, `tuning`, `rowNumbers`, `scrollTo`, `followPanel` (a hand-typed `ProvidedPanels` stops compiling: item (5) above). Every type named in section 6 is exported as a type from index.ts by the entry that first declares it (the resolver entry, type-only; `ViewHandle` stays `PanelReadHandle` until the operations, pin and configure entries widen it to the verbs of section 6), and an entry that adds an exported type or value later (`PanelResult`, `PinResult`, `ResetResult`, `ConfigureResult`, `RowNumbers`, the workspace types) puts index.ts in its touches, and `check:api-report` prints no warning of any kind (ae-forgotten-export, ae-missing-release-tag or any other).

## 11. How phonux-app adopts it
Public API only; the app's hook order and `GoalFrontPage` stay. History becomes the root template (`definePanel({ id: 'history', view: HistoryColumn, settings: { layer: 'raised', enterFrom: 'anchor', title: 'History' } })`) with no header buttons. `web` becomes a `multiple` template (`webTemplate.tsx`, JSX icons) whose view is `LivePanel`; its settings declare the app's two buttons as slots, each with `sx: { color: 'common.white' }`: `size` (order 0: 'Panel size', description the tier, `h.setFormFactor` through phone, tablet, desktop, hidden unless `c.settings.closable`) and `pin` (order 1: 'Pin panel', `pressed` from `c.state.pinned`, `pin()` or `unpin()`); plus `pinningWhenNotLive: 'place-only'` (SPEC 4.8) and `restoreVisibility: 'live'` (a 0.1.0 file's parked panels return live). `createWorkspace({ storage, root, templates, tuning: { initialScroll } })` replaces `initialEntries` and `storage` (app: appApis.ts:91, :99); `rowGesture` stays 'step' (Q18). `initialScroll` decides what a person sees at launch, and the owner ruled it (Q20, 'Right end, newest first'): the app ships `tuning: { initialScroll: 'end' }`, so the newest closable panels are in view at launch and the pinned panels and History start off screen to the left; the library default stays 'start'. The app's value is one constant, `APP_INITIAL_SCROLL = 'end'`, exported by `client/src/desktop/workspace/appWorkspace.ts`. `GoalFrontPage` passes `workspace`, `panels.tuning` and `panels.rowNumbers` to `PanelRow` and `panels.rowScroll`, `panels.tuning` and `panels.rowNumbers` to `Workspace`, as two prop objects built outside the host file; the look sites read the resolved tuning and LivePanel drops its own size and pin buttons.

'Max panels on screen' is RETIRED (Q17): the window is as wide as the app window. Two app entries do it: `app-retire-max-panels-on-screen` first, on phonux 0.1.0, whose `SettingsAPI` and `PanelsInput` still require the four members and `storedMaxPanels`, so `useProvideSettings` returns inert values for them and `appApis.ts` passes `storedMaxPanels: null` until `app-adopt-phonux-0.2-imports` removes them, after which `useProvideSettings` returns only `settingsColumn`. The app deletes the Settings section (`MaxPanelsSection.tsx`, its line in test/phone-boundary.test.ts's VIEW_FILES), the store (`adventureSettings.ts`), the bridge's and preload's `row` member, the two `row:*-max-panels` IPC channels with their main-process functions and the `storedMaxPanels` argument; `useProvideSettings` ends returning only `settingsColumn`. A person's stored choice (userData `max-panels-on-screen.json`) is never read again: `dropRetiredSettings(userDataDir)`, in a new electron-free module `electron/retired-settings.ts` (electron/user-data-store.ts imports electron and sits at its ratchet size), removes that one file when main.ts calls it at launch, and nothing else in userData (migration test). `UsbPhoneToast` (app: UsbPhoneToast.tsx:29) becomes `position: fixed`, or it would scroll away with the row. SPEC.md changes through its section 9 path: 6.1 in the retire entry; 1.1 (which also states the launch position), 2.1, 3.1, 3.3-3.5, 3.7, 5.3, 5.4 and 7.2 in `app-adopt-phonux-0.2-workspace` (3.5 keeps Q1's reveal of a parked panel by the gesture); README.md drops the setting. The adoption is two issues, as the plan: `app-adopt-phonux-0.2-imports` pins the rc, retargets the ten hidden names and the 14 removed aliases the app's tests import, removes the inert 0.1.0 values and the gate's skip branch, and changes no behaviour; `app-adopt-phonux-0.2-workspace` then adopts the workspace (templates, header-button slots, tuning sites, the scrolling row, the launch position, the fixed toast, SPEC and README).
