Evidence required:
1. C1 with F = 88. Allow-list: a new read of `.collapsible` for a permission decision that this entry adds is listed by function name in the allow-list the permission entry created in test/source-invariants.test.ts, in the same commit (the file is in Touches); without it that test goes red in the first entry that lands such a read. This entry adds no value export (C4: the barrel list is unchanged and `git diff --stat -- etc` shows only the PanelWindow fields and the PanelsInput members; no export type is added and check:api-report prints no warning of any kind (ae-forgotten-export, ae-missing-release-tag or any other)); PanelWindow gains `instance?`, `overrides?`, `applied?`, `declared?` (typed in panelWindow.ts). New root files (workspacePlan.ts) raise MIN_LIBRARY_FILES in test/boundary.test.ts in the same commit.
2. Interfaces this entry produces. viewRegistry.tsx gains a minimal template registry: `registerTemplate(t: PanelTemplate): () => void`, `getTemplate(id: string): PanelTemplate | undefined`, `listTemplates(): readonly PanelTemplate[]`; registering a template whose id equals a registerView key throws template-collision; registerView over a template id leaves the template registered and wins for rendering (both orders tested here, including registerView('history') over a template); the component that renders a template view with its handle and manager belongs to the attach entry, so nothing here renders one. PanelsInput gains `templates?: readonly PanelTemplate[]`, `declared?: readonly { id: string; templateId: string; settings?: PanelSettings; params?: JsonValue; url?: string }[]`, `workspaceDefaults?` (the row-numbers entry added it, with the Host-bag create path that uses it) and `tuning?` (a partial WorkspaceTuning, added by the visibility entry), so templates and code-declared panels reach useProvidePanels before createWorkspace exists. workspacePlan.ts exports pure `reconcileWorkspace(i: { saved: unknown; templates: readonly PanelTemplate[]; declared: PanelsInput['declared']; workspaceDefaults?; rootId: string; current: readonly PanelWindow[]; freshId(): string }): { entries: PanelWindow[]; notes: SettingNote[]; dismissed: string[] }`. The recorders, in panelWindow.ts: `recordOverride(entries: readonly PanelWindow[], key: 'anchor' | 'formFactor' | 'parkable', ids: readonly string[]): PanelWindow[]` copies each listed entry's CURRENT value (its anchor, its formFactor, or its collapsible as parkable) into overrides[key], skipping the root, an entry with no settings and an entry whose resolved rememberLayout is false, and returns the same array when nothing changed (so no commit follows); `clearOverrides(entries: readonly PanelWindow[], ids: readonly string[]): PanelWindow[]` sets overrides to {} on each listed entry that carries settings and returns the same array only when every such entry already holds {}. These two and reconcileWorkspace (which copies the saved file's own overrides as read) are the only writers of PanelWindow.overrides. THIS entry calls recordOverride inside the same setPanels updater as the gesture: PanelsAPI.move (reorderPanel in panels/usePanelManager.ts) and PanelsAPI.reanchor (panels/useProvidePanels.ts) with key 'anchor' and every entry id (a drag marks every non-root remembering panel), PanelsAPI.setFormFactor (resizePanel) with key 'formFactor' and the resized id. Later entries add callers and no writer: the operations entry's 'person' verbs go through the same calls; phonux-pin-and-reset adds the callers with key 'parkable' (and 'anchor') for a person's pin and calls clearOverrides from resetLayout. The attach entry builds createWorkspace on these.
3. Pins first in test/pins-persistence.test.ts (committed before source changes, green): the failed-load matrix (null, rejection, throw, invalid file, unknown version: nothing restored, later changes save); StrictMode applies one load; an unmount drops the last change; a throw inside the load success handler leaves the workspace unsettled and saving never resumes (flipped below).
4. 0.1.0 compat: a file written by the vendored 0.1.0 projectWorkspaceState restores with no duplicate singleton, a tablet web panel keeps its tier and a dragged order keeps its order; a Host-bag create({ formFactor: 'desktop' }) panel survives save then a second mount over one in-memory storage; the file this build writes passes the vendored 0.1.0 isWorkspaceState.
5. ### 13. phonux-workspace-attach-and-snapshot (phonux-lib)

**createWorkspace, attach, snapshot, read-only handles, tombstones and the note reporter (no events)**

Intent: createWorkspace and attach on the reconcile and registry interfaces: React-free core, declarative intents replayed at settle, useProvidePanels({ workspace }), read-only handles and the PanelReader, the template adapter, snapshot with PanelState.pinned through a pure isPinned, hooks, late registerTemplate, afterClose tombstones, conflict reporting, memoryWorkspaceStorage.

Interfaces this entry produces (signatures in code; later entries consume them). workspaceCore.ts holds the React-free core behind `WorkspaceOptions = { storage?: WorkspaceStorage; defaults?: Omit(PanelSettings, 'tuning'); root?: PanelTemplate; templates?: readonly PanelTemplate[]; order?: () => readonly PanelId[]; tuning?: WorkspaceTuning; conflicts?: 'warn' | 'throw' | 'off'; newId?: () => string; onError?: (e: unknown) => void }` (exported as a type; every member optional; createWorkspace(o?: WorkspaceOptions) takes it) and `PanelsInput.workspace?: WorkspaceHandle` (when set, `storage` and `initialEntries` beside it are the development errors of bullet 5; the field is the only way a workspace reaches useProvidePanels) and `WorkspaceHandle = { readonly rootId: string; readonly panels: PanelManager; readonly tuning: ResolvedWorkspaceTuning; readonly status: 'detached' | 'loading' | 'ready' | 'disposed'; addPanel(t: PanelTemplate | string, o?: { id?: PanelId; settings?: PanelSettings; params?: JsonValue; url?: string }): PanelHandle; registerTemplate(t: PanelTemplate): () => void; dispose(): void }`. panelHandles.ts: the read-only PanelHandle members here are `id`, `templateId`, `getState(): PanelState`, `getSettings()` and `subscribe(l: () => void): () => void`; PanelState, PanelSnapshot, ViewHandle and PanelReader are the type-only declarations of the resolver entry and are implemented here; `PanelReader = { get(id: PanelId): the read handle or undefined; list(f?): readonly PanelState[]; getSnapshot(): PanelSnapshot; subscribe(l: () => void): () => void }`. panelManager.ts (created here) holds the PanelManager implementation with get, list, getSnapshot and subscribe only; the operations, pin, configure and reset entries add their methods to it. The engine-controls object behind the module-private WeakMap is typed EngineControls in workspaceCore.ts, read only by panelManager.ts and panelHandles.ts, and not exported.
3. Template adapter and context (this entry owns them). panelViewContext.ts (a leaf) holds `PanelViewContext`, carrying `{ handleFor(id: string): ViewHandle | undefined; manager: PanelReader | undefined }`, which PanelRow provides from its workspace prop; viewRegistry.tsx gains `templateViewFor(t: PanelTemplate): ViewComponent`, which reads that context and renders t.view with panel, handle and manager. Mount test: a template registered with registerTemplate and added with addPanel renders through it; t.view receives panel (a PanelSummary), handle (Object.keys equal id, templateId, getState, getSettings, subscribe: the read-only five) and manager (Object.keys equal get, list, getSnapshot, subscribe); registerTemplate then registerView with the same id renders the plain view; registerView then registerTemplate throws template-collision; mutant: the adapter passes no handle; red.

5. Lifecycle: StrictMode applies one load; remount with and without storage; a second hook on an attached workspace, storage or initialEntries beside workspace, and a workspace swapped while mounted are development errors (the swap names the cure, the first workspace stays attached, the new one's handles answer not-attached). Pre-mount drain: console.error captured during the declarative replay equals []; control: a deliberate flushSync from a lifecycle in the same harness is captured. status becomes 'ready' on the same commit that raises the restore-settled flag initialScroll waits for (a deferred-load test reads both in one render).
6. Snapshot: getSnapshot() is reference-equal across renders when nothing changed and its Object.keys are exactly version, status and panels (the PanelSnapshot declaration in phonux-settings-catalog-and-resolver's type block, under that issue's Research, Local insights); each PanelState's Object.keys are exactly id, templateId, status, held, engineId, index, title, url, params, formFactor, listed, settings, pinned and deferredPin, with engineId and index null for a pending or closed panel; reader.list({ status: 'live', listed: true }) filters by those two ListFilter fields; version increments exactly once per commit that changed state; handles are valid before mount (pending), live after attach, still answer getState after unmount; getSettings returns the resolver output (value, source with a SourceLayer word per catalog name, conflicts, notes); PanelState.held is true exactly for a parked panel with a hold; subscribe fires once per change. Errors: an id-less intent for a multiple template throws instance-id-required; an explicit id equal to a registered template id or to 'all', or containing '~', throws invalid-options (instance ids have no length cap beyond the template-id rule of 1-48 characters, so a 49-character id also throws).
7. Tombstones (two launches over one storage): a declared stay-closed panel closed through PanelsAPI.close or cleared by clearAll is absent next launch; return-next-launch is recreated and absent from dismissed; the 256 cap drops the oldest; detach does not read an unmount as a mass close; a key both saved and dismissed keeps the saved entry; a tombstone for a template now closable:false is deleted; an undo removes it. Late registration (S2): registerTemplate over a placeholder turns a saved closable:false entry into a template-built locked one; hostCreatable, multiple and maxInstances hold on addPanel and late registration; an over-limit addPanel returns a handle in status closed and does not throw.

Mutants: isPinned reads a stored flag; isPinned reads the placement of an (A) half; red.

Engine controls reach the manager only through a module-private WeakMap: a barrel test fails if any export name matches /controls/i, with a control (a scratch copy exporting getEngineControls turns it red); the api report check follows.

No events and no flush: `grep -rn 'PanelEvent' *.ts *.tsx panels | wc -l` prints 0 while `grep -rln 'PanelHandle' *.ts *.tsx panels | wc -l` is above 0 (control); the barrel list has no flush export. Every verb takes an internal `source: 'code' | 'person'` last parameter (default 'code') that the header-button entry and the pin entry pass; `PanelOpSource` is exported from panelManager.ts for them; the ViewHandle a view receives is built here with exactly the panel's own verbs (show, hide, toggleVisibility, close, open, move, place, setFormFactor, scrollTo, pin and unpin arrive with the pin entry, getState, getSettings, subscribe, and a configure arriving with the configure entry); scrollTo calls ProvidedPanels.scrollTo from the scroller entry (a boolean: true when a request was recorded) and the manager verbs call ProvidedPanels.followPanel(id: string): void; panelManager.ts already holds get, list, getSnapshot and subscribe from the attach entry and gains the verbs here.

Outcomes table (design 7.2, quoted under Research, Local insights) driven as data: ops (show, hide, toggle, close, open, move, place, setFormFactor, scrollTo) x states (live, parked, closed, pending) x settings variants with literal expected results; executed === the computed product and >= 60. Manager-visible matrix: for each of closable, parkable (through hide), draggable, crossable, placeable, resizable, formFactors, hideStickiness, hideWhenLast, toggleWhenClosed, showWhenClosed, startVisibility, multiple, maxInstances, placeOnAdd, placeOnShow a case runs value A and value B through the handle and asserts the documented result (toggleWhenClosed and showWhenClosed are owned here as differentials over their values and the closed, live and parked states, in test/panel-manager-operations.test.ts, whose basename contains 'operations', the owner word of the configure-matrix entry's coverage-source test).
5. Refusals touch nothing: for every refusal entries is reference-equal, no setPanels call, no extra commit (render counter), snapshot version unchanged.
6. Busy contract with literal rows, no escape hatch. On [A B C] (all live, H root omitted): close A + show B = busy (close excludes every other operation on any panel in its turn); close A + hide B = busy; close A + open B = busy; close A + close B = busy; hide B then close A = busy; hide A + hide B = ok, both parked (and with hideWhenLast refuse on a two-panel row [A B] the second answers would-empty-row); show B + hide C = ok; setFormFactor A + hide A = busy. The same table is run from a discrete handler, a timer, a promise continuation and a passive effect and must give identical results in all four (a differing trigger fails the test; the gate is fixed, never the table). `resetLayout('all')` and `configureTuning({ rowGap })` are busy while any id holds a token.
7. Raw-mutator gate: close, undoClose, activate, move, reanchor and setFormFactor each take a raw token on the ids they touch; clearAll and create take no token and are never no-ops; a manager call on the same id in the same turn is busy; two raw calls on one id behave as the pins; a raw call on an id holding a manager token is a no-op; mutant: raw mutators take no token; red.
8. hideWhenLast with the turn's in-flight set: hide A + hide B and hide A + close B on [A B] leave at least one panel present; refused only when none would remain; root-less and fixed-column cases. toggleVisibility: live to hide, parked to show, closed ignore and reopen for both values; not-parkable and not-closable on fixed columns. show() on a closed declared panel: refuse gives is-closed, reopen re-adds at placeOnAdd (instance-limit when full); a closed anonymous instance answers is-closed under both values.
9. open() inside the undo window then undoClose: key unique after both (S1); undoClose skips and does not report the closed key; the window record is dropped when open() re-adds the key. Closed records: a declared panel keeps its record and open() re-adds it; an anonymous closed instance answers is-closed; 1000 add and close cycles leave getSnapshot().panels.length at most declared + live. reapply: usePanelClosing gains an optional parameter `reapply?: (panel: PanelWindow) => { panel: PanelWindow; placeAs: 'old-slot' | AddPlacement }`, default `(p) => ({ panel: p, placeAs: 'old-slot' })` (the 0.1.0 identity); with a workspace attached, useProvidePanels passes one that rebuilds the closed entry through applyTemplate(reconfigure) from the core record's current layers and returns the panel's resolved undoPlacement as placeAs; undoClose restores the returned panel at its recorded index when placeAs is 'old-slot' (today's restorePanel call) and otherwise restores it with its live flag kept and then re-places it through resolvePlacement/placePanel from the placement entry (panelPlacement.ts, fallback placeFallback); without a workspace the 0.1.0 pure spread is unchanged; a manager verb never edits the ClosedView list (reference-equal).
10. Source, views and scroll wiring: after a 'code' call no entry's overrides has changed, and after a 'person' call the effect of recordOverride (the saved-overrides entry's function in panelWindow.ts) is present: overrides.anchor after move, place or reanchor and overrides.formFactor after setFormFactor, exactly as PanelsAPI's own gestures record them; Object.keys of the ViewHandle equal the listed verbs. scrollTo: live records a scroll request (changed true, or false when already there or while a drag holds the row) whatever scrollOnMove says, parked answers not-live, closed is-closed, pending is-pending, and it is never busy. scrollOnMove: move() on an in-view panel records a follow request and the panel ends inside the window (literal scrollLeft); place() and setFormFactor() follow through the raw wrappers of the scroller entry; an out-of-view panel records none; scrollOnMove none records none; mutant: move() records no follow request; red. open() on a closed declared panel, and show() on a parked, hidden or out-of-view live panel, each record one scrollOnOpen request and the row scrolls to the literal target of that setting (explicit 'into-view', 'start' and 'center' each; one default case reads LIBRARY_PANEL_DEFAULTS.scrollOnOpen); show() of a live panel in view records none (changed:false), hide() records none and scrollOnOpen none records none; mutant: show() records no request; red. S3 through the manager: at least 500 seeded sequences over random settings vectors, each guarantee of design 7.3 (S1 to S10, quoted under Research, Local insights) checked after every step; printed and asserted 'sequences run: N' with N >= 500.
11. The owner example with its toggle runs as a mount test: `const toggle = () => one.toggleVisibility()` called from a button in panel two's view (a closure over the full handle) hides and shows panel one. The close-order KNOWN-BAD pin is renamed LIMIT: and a manager test shows busy prevents it; `grep -rhoE "test\(.KNOWN-BAD:[0-9]" test | sort -u | wc -l` (distinct pin titles, so a pinned string repeated inside one pin counts once) prints 0. No value export beyond the attach entry's; every type that an exported signature names (PanelResult, PanelRefusal, PanelOpSource and the PanelHandle members' types) is either exported as a type from index.ts or tagged @internal as the report's existing convention requires, so the report has no ae-forgotten-export and no ae-missing-release-tag; the type PanelResult is added to index.ts, which is in Touches for this reason (the other result types belong to the pin and configure entries) and check:api-report prints no warning of any kind (ae-forgotten-export, ae-missing-release-tag or any other). C2 and C3.
12. C5 with N = 8: show() records no scroll request; busy gate off; refusal checked after the engine call; toggleWhenClosed values swapped; hideWhenLast ignoring the in-flight set; raw mutators without a token; close not exclusive; move() recording no follow request.

Reference files (a worker or verifier that cannot open them stops): the whole design, /Users/joe/git/phonUX.worktrees/_goalfrontpage-workflow/DESIGN-phonux-0.2-rev7.md, and the settings catalog, /Users/joe/git/phonUX.worktrees/_goalfrontpage-workflow/DESIGN-phonux-0.2-rev7-catalog.json. Any design section a bullet names and this issue does not quote is read there.

Design section 12, verbatim (C1 to C5 are what a bullet means by "C1", "C2 and C3", "C4" or "C5"):

*Common evidence (every library entry; values in its first bullet; the shared tools are built in the scaffold entry, whose evidence carries their contracts; scratch space is mktemp -d outside the repo).* C1 BASE from tools/testcount.sh before any edit and AFTER at least BASE + F. C2 tools/comment-rule.sh exits 0 with files above 0. C3 tools/ci-chain.sh exits 0. C4 for each value export added: the barrel test's list gains exactly those names (compared with Object.keys(index)), api-report:update then check:api-report exit 0 and git diff --stat -- etc is non-empty; for each type added to index.ts the entry lists the names and check:api-report prints no warning of any kind (ae-forgotten-export, ae-missing-release-tag or any other); none, and the diff is empty, otherwise; a field, prop or signature change is named in the entry and appears in the report diff. C5 mutants: node tools/mutate-check.mjs tools/mutants/ENTRY.json --planned N (ENTRY is the entry's id). A descriptor's differential is differential(NAME, ...) in test/support/differential.ts; the global gate greps test/*.test.ts for that literal.

*Barrel test.* Its title carries the count ('exactly the 43 runtime values, no more, no fewer'). The entry that adds value exports edits the title and the sorted list in the same commit, renaming and never deleting the test: resolver 50, permissions 51, look tuning 54, keepMounted 55, row numbers 56, attach 60, header buttons 62; the release-candidate entry asserts 62 and edits no title. Every other entry leaves the file's title and list alone.

*Brief rule for every worker (both repos' CLAUDE.md):* comments say why, a constraint the code cannot show; no provenance (ticket or queue ids, dates, owner names); past-bug stories go to CHANGELOG.md or the commit message; design notes go in DESIGN.md, never a link to a tracker. Pins live in the entry that changes the behaviour, committed with its first tests; a flipped pin is renamed, never deleted.

Design 7.2 and 7.3, verbatim (the outcomes table bullet 4 drives and the guarantees bullet 10 checks):

**Design 7.2 Outcomes (operation by state)**
| op | live | parked | closed | pending |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| show | scrolls per scrollOnOpen if out of view; changed:true only when a scroll was dispatched | placeOnShow, activate (may park `park` panels), scroll | showWhenClosed: refuse = `is-closed`; reopen = open() | `is-pending` |
| hide | parkable else `not-parkable`; `hideWhenLast:'refuse'` with no other panel left once this turn's hides and closes count: `would-empty-row`; else park, hold per hideStickiness | hold per hideStickiness | `is-closed` | `is-pending` |
| toggle | hide | show | per toggleWhenClosed | `is-pending` |
| close | closable else `not-closable` | same | ok, unchanged | `is-pending` |
| open | ok, unchanged | ok, unchanged | declared: re-add at placeOnAdd and scroll per scrollOnOpen (`instance-limit` when full); anonymous: `is-closed` | `is-pending` |
| move | draggable else `not-draggable` | `not-live` | `is-closed` | `is-pending` |
| place | placeable else `not-placeable` | places, stays parked | `is-closed` | `is-pending` |
| setFormFactor | resizable else `not-resizable`; tier in formFactors else `tier-not-allowed` | same, stays parked | `is-closed` | `is-pending` |
| scrollTo | scrolls (`changed:false` if already there, or while a drag holds the row) | `not-live` | `is-closed` | `is-pending` |
| pin, unpin | applies the half (6A) | per pinningWhenNotLive | declared: apply-on-next-show stores on the record, else `is-closed` | `is-pending` |

An accepted move, place, setFormFactor, pin or unpin on a live panel then follows `scrollOnMove` (6B). `configure` and `resetLayout` follow 7.6 and 7.7. `disposed`, `not-attached` and `not-ready` refuse every imperative call; only `addPanel` queues.

**Design 7.3 Invariants a test can pin**
- S0 resolver: pure; forced rules hold over 1000 random layer stacks; `conflicts` lists exactly what a template, instance or host layer asked otherwise.
- S1 row: ids and keys unique, order stable, one root; after every SETTLED commit (a window change, `tuning.rowGap` change or `parkable` flip settles in two) on a measured row the live `park` panels fit the window beside the non-`park` panels except the floor (F19). A refusal leaves `entries` reference-equal with no commit.
- S2 every setting holds on every creation path (restore alone may exceed `multiple` and `maxInstances`). S3 at least 500 random sequences over random settings vectors: no restricting setting is bypassed and show() always works.
- S5 two operations on one panel in one turn give `busy` for the second; a close is exclusive (7.4). S6 restore(save(x)) is stable over two launches; a changed default reaches untouched panels only; the person's overrides survive any configure; `deferredPin` is never saved.
- S7 every name has a doc row, enforcement site, owner entry, `on_configure` class and a differential test; a test that ran no scenario fails the build. S8 one probe per catalog row and a per-class status table; S9 reset lands declared panels in their literal default arrangement, a second call is `changed:false`; S10 `pin()` then `unpin()` restores row order, History listing and permissions for every valid definition, and a drag of an unrelated panel leaves the read unchanged.
- S11 scroll: with every panel 'scroll', over 500 random sequences no panel is ever parked for room; a row wider than the window has `computeRowNumbers` stripWidth above windowWidth and `rowMarginLeft` 0 (the real `scrollWidth` is the real-layout gate's: jsdom has no layout); after an open with `scrollOnOpen` 'into-view' the panel lies inside the window; `park` panels alone are ever parked for room.
- S12 edge scroll: with `scrollOnDrag` 'none' a held drag never changes scrollLeft; with 'edge' the released offset is the pointer offset plus the row's scroll distance, scrollLeft never exceeds the strip's bound, the box lands on its slot; the scroller's claim is held only while a drag is.

### 15. phonux-pin-and-reset (phonux-lib)

**pin(), unpin(), pinningWhenNotLive, deferred pins, the person's pin override and resetLayout**

Intent: pin() and unpin() apply a readable pinning definition in one commit (place then fix; free then place); pinningWhenNotLive and deferredPin decide what a parked, hidden or closed panel does; the person's pin is saved as overrides.parkable; a fixed pin never needs room; resetLayout clears overrides and deferred pins and re-places by default.

Prerequisites: phonux-panel-handle-operations

Touches:
- panelPin.ts
- panelReset.ts
- panelManager.ts
- panelHandles.ts
- workspaceCore.ts
- panelWindow.ts
- panels/useProvidePanels.ts
- panels/usePanelManager.ts
- panels/usePanelClosing.ts
- test/panel-pin.test.ts
- test/panel-pin-deferred.test.ts
- test/panel-pin-property.test.ts
- test/panel-reset.test.ts
- test/boundary.test.ts
- tools/mutants/phonux-pin-and-reset.json
- etc/*.api.md
- index.ts
- test/source-invariants.test.ts

Evidence required:
1. C1 with F = 82. Allow-list: a new read of `.collapsible` for a permission decision that this entry adds is listed by function name in the allow-list the permission entry created in test/source-invariants.test.ts, in the same commit (the file is in Touches); without it that test goes red in the first entry that lands such a read. Sizing: pin and reset stay one pass because resetLayout reuses this entry's placement and calls clearOverrides from the saved-overrides entry (panelWindow.ts) for its override clearing, never writing overrides itself; if it fails by measure the coordinator splits at pin, unpin, pinningWhenNotLive and deferredPin against resetLayout. Difficulty: hard (the deferred pin is injected into the updater of every path that makes a panel live). PanelWindow gains `deferredPin?: { by: 'pin' | 'unpin' } | { parkable: boolean }` (session only, typed in panelWindow.ts). New root files (panelReset.ts) raise MIN_LIBRARY_FILES in test/boundary.test.ts in the same commit.
2. Interfaces this entry produces, importable by the header-button and configure entries (a typecheck-only scratch import proves it): panelPin.ts exports `decidePinChange(entry: PanelWindow, status: 'live' | 'parked' | 'hidden' | 'closed-declared' | 'undo' | 'pending' | 'closed-anonymous', setting: PinningWhenNotLive, change: { by: 'pin' | 'unpin' } | { parkable: boolean }): { kind: 'apply' | 'show' | 'defer' | 'refuse'; code?: PanelRefusal }`. There is no room check and no 'no-room' code. pin() and unpin() are added to PanelHandle and ViewHandle taking the source argument, returning `PinResult = PanelResult & { shown?: boolean; deferred?: boolean }` (PanelResult is the operations entry's type); `resetLayout(target: PanelId | 'all'): ResetResult` is added to PanelManager with `ResetResult = PanelResult & { panels?: readonly PanelId[]; parked?: readonly PanelId[]; returned?: readonly PanelId[] }`; `pin(): PinResult; unpin(): PinResult` are the method signatures.
3. pin() and unpin() under the default definition: results equal today's reanchor results over literal rows (pin lands left of the root past already pinned panels, unpin returns to the far right), every other entry field (collapsible, locked, listed, formFactor) is identical, PanelState.pinned follows, a call already in the target state is changed:false; a drag of an unrelated panel after a pin leaves PanelState.pinned unchanged for the (A) definition, the (B) default, a named-place (A) definition and the combined one, in a rooted and a root-less row; mutant: pinned read from a stored flag; red.
4. Order and reachability (S10): for each of at least 6 valid definitions (default; the parkable pair alone; the pair with { after: 'dock' } and row-end; row-start with after-fixed; the pair with row-start and row-end; the combined one) pin() then unpin() returns row order, panelList listing and the placeable, draggable, listed, clearable values to the start (literal arrays and booleans), and between the calls the pinned panel is a fixed column that unpin can still place; mutant: unpin places before it frees; red (unpin answers not-placeable).
5. All or nothing: an explicit placeable:false with a placement half answers not-placeable and parkable is unchanged; a fixed pin on a tablet panel answers tier-not-allowed; an explicit resizable:true under a fixed pin answers settings-conflict (the conflict has key 'resizable', kind 'forced', rule 'C1' and forcedBy 'parkable', and the result's setting is 'resizable'); each leaves entries reference-equal with no commit; mutant: pin applies parkable before the placement check; red.
6. A fixed pin never needs room: a type probe shows 'no-room' is not a PanelRefusal member; at a measured 800 px container with History and three fixed pins in an all-scroll row every pin is accepted and computeRowNumbers gives a stripWidth above the windowWidth (jsdom has no layout, so the real scrollWidth is the real-layout gate's); in a row holding park panels the same pins are accepted and the settled row parks the fewest park panels (liveParkable within the floored capacity) or overflows and scrolls; an unmeasured row accepts them.
7. Pin follows the person (container geometry stubbed with literals; scrollOnMove set explicitly to 'follow' except the default case, which reads LIBRARY_PANEL_DEFAULTS.scrollOnMove): eleven panels at a measured 1000 px container with the last web panel in view at scrollLeft 3482: pin() (row-start) leaves the panel at left 16 and the row scrolls to scrollLeft 0; unpin() (row-end) puts it back at left 4076 and the row scrolls to 3482; a code pin() on an out-of-view panel scrolls nothing; scrollOnMove none scrolls nothing; resetLayout(id) of an in-view panel follows and resetLayout('all') scrolls nothing; mutant: pin() records no follow request; red.
8. pinningWhenNotLive place-only on an engine-parked (K) and a hidden (H) panel: a placement-only half applies in place and the panel stays parked or held, equal to reanchor on a parked panel today; a half naming parkable answers would-reshape-row, entries reference-equal; closed declared and undo window answer is-closed; pending, closed anonymous and same turn answer is-pending, is-closed, busy. refuse: the whole pin answers would-reshape-row. show: a hidden panel's hold is released and the show path runs (the half's placement replaces placeOnShow), the half applies and the result is shown:true, all in one commit; no flushSync import in the new files (a planted import turns the source check red). Per-status object: { hidden: 'refuse', parked: 'place-only' } differs for a hidden and an engine-parked panel in one row.
9. apply-on-next-show: the call stores { by } and changes no other entry field, result deferred:true; a call whose result equals what the panel reads now cancels it, otherwise the last call wins; it applies in the same commit as the live flip on each path: show(), a History click, a Host-bag activate, the row gesture (placement first, then the park choice and enter side from the landing slot, literal arrays), an automatic return and an undo; each leaves deferredPin null; for a declared closed panel the deferral is stored on the record and open() applies it. Clears and drops: a live pin() or unpin() clears it; a close of the parked entry drops it; resetLayout clears it (the two configure-driven cases, configure({ parkable }) clearing a deferral and configure({ pinning }) keeping its direction, need the configure verb and are the configure entry's bullets, not tested here); a deferral whose tier, placeable or conflict check fails at apply is dropped with a runtime note and the show still happens; the saved projection never contains it; a deferred parkable:false applied through open() or undo on a record with live:false yields live:true; mutant: live not normalised; red.
10. The person's pin: the person-source path (a press, from the header-button entry) records overrides.anchor and, for a half naming parkable, overrides.parkable in the saved projection and writes nothing to instance; this entry adds those callers to recordOverride (panelWindow.ts, from the saved-overrides entry: key 'parkable' with the pinned panel's id, and key 'anchor') and adds no other writer of overrides, so the saved-overrides entry's source check stays green; a code pin() records neither; over two launches, for a declared and an anonymous panel with the combined definition, the person's fixed pin restores fixed, reads pinned and the declaration's parkable does not win; rememberLayout:false records nothing and carries stored overrides dormant.
11. resetLayout, literal rows: (a) declared A B C with placeOnAdd row-end, dragged to [C A B]: resetLayout('all') gives [A B C]; (b) one id re-places only that panel and clears only its overrides; (c) a tablet panel the person resized returns to its default tier, a tier that no longer fits parks the fewest park panels and a freed slot returns a stacked panel, the result listing parked and returned (a scrolling row parks none); (d) a person-pinned fixed column becomes parkable again and its overrides are {} in the file; (e) parked and hidden panels are re-placed and stay parked or held. Statuses: closed declared clears overrides so open() places by default; undo window marks the record reset; closed anonymous is-closed for one id, skipped by 'all'; pending is-pending, 'all' before ready not-ready; a stored deferredPin is cleared; the file has overrides {} and applied.placeOnAdd for every named entry, a second launch keeps the reset row, a second call is changed:false, a call that only clears a stored non-empty override is changed:true; busy while any panel is in flight; 'all' is rejected as a template id and an instance id.
12. S3 and S10 property (guarantees S3 and S10 of design 7.3, quoted under Research, Local insights): at least 500 seeded sequences over random settings vectors and valid definitions including pin, unpin, drags of unrelated panels, deferred pins, resetLayout and the operations; printed and asserted 'sequences run: N' with N >= 500, each guarantee of 7.3 checked after every step. One differential per name pinning and pinningWhenNotLive, in test/panel-pin.test.ts (basename contains 'pin', the configure-matrix entry's coverage word); covered set equals those two. No value export beyond the attach entry's; the types PinResult and ResetResult are added to index.ts (in Touches for this reason) and check:api-report prints no warning of any kind (ae-forgotten-export, ae-missing-release-tag or any other).
13. Scroll requests of the show paths (S11): a pin() under pinningWhenNotLive 'show' on a hidden panel, and a deferred pin applied by show(), open(), an undo or a History click, each record exactly one request, the panel's scrollOnOpen (a spy on ProvidedPanels.scrollTo and followPanel sees one request in total, not a second follow request) and the row scrolls to the literal target; an in-view panel records none; scrollOnOpen none records none.
14. C5 with N = 9: isPinned reads a stored flag; unpin places before it frees; pin applies parkable before the placement check; pin() records no follow request; a deferred pin skipped by an automatic return; deferredPin not cleared by a live pin(); reset leaves overrides.parkable in the file; reset leaves deferredPin; a code pin recorded as the person's. C2 and C3.

### 16. phonux-header-buttons (phonux-lib)

**Header buttons as generic slots: the developer declares icon, label and click function**

Intent: Owner Q14. headerButtons: slots keyed by id with icon, label, onClick(selfHandle, ctx), side, order, pressed, description, tooltip, disabled, hidden, sx. Panel draws MUI IconButtons in the title bar; usePanelHeaderButtons and PanelHeaderButtons serve views with their own header; a press is the person's gesture. No built-in button; none declared, Panel markup equals the golden.

### 17. phonux-panel-configure (phonux-lib)

**configure: the three calls, gate, refusals, writer, undo, doors and views**

Intent: configure(id), configureTemplate and configureTuning as separate calls (a key at the wrong call is wrong-scope): re-resolve through the one resolver, refuse with typed codes, apply the transition class in ONE commit through the in-flight gate with no flushSync, applyTemplate(reconfigure) as the only writer, undo through reapply. The per-setting matrix is the next entry.

Prerequisites: phonux-pin-and-reset, phonux-header-buttons

Touches:
- panelConfigure.ts
- panelManager.ts
- panelHandles.ts
- panelPin.ts
- workspaceCore.ts
- panelSettings.ts
- panels/useProvidePanels.ts
- panels/usePanelManager.ts
- panels/usePanelClosing.ts
- PanelRow.tsx
- test/panel-configure-core.test.ts
- test/panel-configure-persistence.test.ts
- test/panel-configure-doors.test.ts
- test/source-invariants.test.ts
- test/boundary.test.ts
- tools/mutants/phonux-panel-configure.json
- etc/*.api.md
- DESIGN.md
- index.ts

Evidence required:
1. C1 with F = 94 (90 for the bullets below as first written plus the 4 tests of the two bullets (12 and 13) added before the C5 bullet: the parkable deferral, the pinning deferral, the headerButtons NOW case and the parked-panel case). ConfigureResult (design section 6) carries, on success, changed, shown, now (the setting names applied in this commit), later (applied at the next use), shadowed (names a person's override still wins) and notes (SettingNote: code, panelId?, key?, message), and, on refusal, ok false, code, message and setting? (the catalog name of the offending key: for settings-conflict it equals the SettingConflict key, for wrong-scope and not-view-settable the key asked); a patched key reads source 'instance' in getSettings(). Allow-list: a new read of `.collapsible` for a permission decision that this entry adds is listed by function name in the allow-list the permission entry created in test/source-invariants.test.ts, in the same commit (the file is in Touches); without it that test goes red in the first entry that lands such a read. No value export beyond the attach entry's, and the type ConfigureResult is added to index.ts (in Touches for this reason) with check:api-report printing no warning of any kind (ae-forgotten-export, ae-missing-release-tag or any other); the typed signatures are in the design (section 6). New root files (panelConfigure.ts) raise MIN_LIBRARY_FILES in test/boundary.test.ts in the same commit.
2. Class laws: a refusal leaves entries reference-equal, no setPanels call, no extra commit (render counter) and an unchanged snapshot version; a NEXT key changes only that panel's stored settings (every other field of every entry reference-equal, nothing parked, shown, moved or scrolled); a NOW key changes only the named fields; an equal patch is changed:false with no commit; a two-key patch whose second key is refused applies neither; a state change planted between the call and the commit (test hook on the render array) makes the updater return prev.
3. Writer: applyTemplate(reconfigure) exact delta for three keys per class on a person-resized tablet panel and on a dragged panel: formFactor, size, anchor, live (bar the live:true a flip to parkable:false sets), hostData, key, overrides and applied are reference-equal; source-invariants asserts the single writer covers both modes (planted second writer: red). Mutant: reconfigure writes formFactor from settings; red.
4. Closed and undo-window records, literal: configure closable:false inside a declared panel's window then undoClose gives locked:true and close() refused not-closable; configure title then undo restores the new title; configure undoPlacement then undo places by it; resetLayout then undo restores at placeOnAdd with the resolved tier; a closed declared panel past the window takes the edit and open() uses it; the ClosedView objects are reference-equal before and after every configure; an anonymous closed instance answers is-closed.
5. Raw API in the same turn, both orders: raw setFormFactor then configure formFactors narrowed = busy; configure then raw setFormFactor = the raw call is a no-op and the tier unchanged; raw close then configure(closable:false) = busy; configure(closable:false) then raw close = no close; raw clearAll then configure on a removed id = busy; configure and manager calls on different panels combine; configure after a close in the same turn = busy (a close is exclusive).
6. Conflicts and scope: a forced-rule conflict on a named key (closable:false with clearable:true) and on an unnamed key a forced rule would change (viewState reset-on-park beside keepMounted true) is settings-conflict (the refusal's setting equals the conflict's key, 'clearable' for the first and 'keepMounted' for the second, and its message names the conflict's rule and forcedBy: C2 with 'closable', C6 with 'viewState'); a conflict that already existed before the call does not refuse (control); formFactors narrowed below the current tier is tier-not-allowed and widened is ok; multiple true->false with 2 instances and maxInstances 5->1 with 3 live give instance-limit with nothing closed, 1->5 is ok; configureTemplate and configureTuning apply to future creations and to every panel's resolved tuning.
7. Doors (Q11) through the manager: a hand-written table of the three calls x key classes (instance key, template key, workspace-only tuning key, per-panel tuning leaf): configure(id) accepts instance keys and the four per-panel tuning leaves (dragToClose, slide, hopFraction, edgeScroll) and answers wrong-scope for template keys and the seven workspace-only keys; configureTemplate accepts only hostCreatable, multiple, maxInstances and answers wrong-scope for every other known key; configureTuning accepts the eleven tuning keys (the per-panel four set the workspace default) and answers wrong-scope for instance and template keys; an unknown key or template throws; executed === calls x classes x one key per class; a wrong-scope refusal leaves entries reference-equal; a typecheck probe: configure(id, { hostCreatable: true }) fails to compile (InstancePatch) and the cast call answers wrong-scope; mutant: configure forwards a template key; red.
8. Views: a template view's own handle accepts title, listed, layer, formFactor, placeOnAdd { anchor } and enterFrom 'anchor' and answers not-view-settable (entries reference-equal) for closable, parkable, keepMounted, overflow, headerButtons, pinning and every other key; the PanelReader has no configure; a view cannot reach configureTemplate, configureTuning or resetLayout (Object.keys of both props asserted); a view that tries handle.configure({ closable: false }) leaves the panel closable.
9. Q2 persistence, three launches over one memory storage: rememberLayout true->false->true with a dragged and a resized panel (overrides survive and apply again); resizable:false after the person resized to tablet (tier tablet in-session and next launch); formFactors narrowed to exclude a saved tier (dropped with a note); persist saved->session (live anchor untouched, an explicit rememberLayout:true is settings-conflict, dependents get derived anchors); configure on an anonymous instance (title, listed, tuning.slide) restores configured, the same on a declared panel does not (the declaration wins); configureTemplate and configureTuning are not saved; the file's overrides are unchanged by any configure; configure of formFactor or parkable while the person's override exists reports shadowed; a deferred pin is never in the file.
10. Turn and lifecycle: configure and any operation on the same panel in one handler give busy for the second across handler, timer, promise and effect triggers (identical results, as in the operations entry); configure from a layout effect, a passive effect, a timer and a promise commits once with console.error captured equal to [] (control: a deliberate flushSync from a lifecycle is captured); source-invariants asserts no flushSync import in the configure files (planted import: red). configureTuning({ rowGap }): busy while any id holds a token; re-prices the row in one commit; in park mode a capacity drop parks the fewest park panels through the reconcile effect (liveParkable at most capacity, asserted after the settled commit); a scrolling row only re-prices and clamps scrollLeft; unmeasured: stored.
11. Result object: now, later, shadowed and shown for a sample of 20 patches equal the expected literals written by hand; getSettings() after a configure deep-equals resolvePanelSettings over the patched layers with source 'instance' for the patched keys; null removes the key and restores the cascade value and its source; a host-asked key (title via the Host-bag) is restored by null. S3 extension: the generator adds configure of a random legal patch and resetLayout; sequences run >= 500 (printed and asserted), 7.3 checked after every step.
12. deferredPin and configure (cases that need the verb, moved here from phonux-pin-and-reset), literal rows: (1) a parked panel holding deferredPin { by: 'pin' } (stored by pin() under pinningWhenNotLive 'apply-on-next-show'): configure(id, { parkable: true }) answers ok, deferredPin reads null before and after the next show(), and show() applies no deferred pin (a spy on the placement path sees none); (2) the same parked panel with deferredPin { by: 'pin' }: configure(id, { pinning }) with a new definition whose pinned half names placement { after: 'dock' } keeps deferredPin { by: 'pin' } (the stored direction) and the next show() applies the NEW definition, so the panel lands right of 'dock', not at the old placement.
13. configure({ headerButtons }) behaviour (moved here from phonux-header-buttons), literal rows: a live panel with a focused header button: configure(id, { headerButtons: { b: false } }) is NOW, so the slot is gone in the same commit, document.activeElement is the panel's header row, which carries tabindex -1, and never body; configure(id, { headerButtons: { b: { icon, label: 'B', onClick } } }) makes the slot appear in the same commit; a changed label shows at the commit; on a parked panel the value is stored (getSettings().value.headerButtons has it, shown false for the layout) and the buttons draw when the panel returns by show(); the view's own handle answering not-view-settable stays in bullet 8. Each case in bullets 12 and 13 asserts a before value and an after value written as literals (deferredPin null or the stored object, the landing index, activeElement's tag and tabindex attribute); the configure-matrix probes assert the per-setting before and after observable but not these side effects, so these bullets are their only pin.
14. C5 with N = 8: a NEXT setting applied by moving a live panel; refusal checked after the commit; the settings-conflict diff compared against an empty baseline; a two-key patch applied key by key; the busy gate bypassed; reconfigure writing the live tier; configure forwarding a template key to the template layer; configure not exclusive after a close. ### 18. phonux-configure-matrix (phonux-lib)

**configure matrix: a per-class status table and one probe per catalog row**

Intent: Tests only: the transition rule of every one of the 61 descriptors is pinned by a hand-written 36-cell class table (4 classes x 9 statuses) plus one hand-written probe per row with its before and after observable, the owner's literal transition cases, and the S7 coverage sources.

Prerequisites: phonux-panel-configure

- test/support/configure-matrix.ts
- test/panel-configure-matrix.test.ts
- test/panel-configure-cases.test.ts
- test/panel-configure-property.test.ts
- test/settings-coverage-sources.test.ts
- tools/mutants/phonux-configure-matrix.json

Evidence required:
1. C1 with F = 127 (one test per row = 61, 36 class cells, 20 owner cases, 10 other). Scope: `git diff --name-only $(git merge-base HEAD main) | grep -vE '^(test|tools)/' | wc -l` prints 0 and the list is non-empty. A red probe stops the entry: it posts a comment naming the probe and its owning entry and the coordinator blocks it; nothing is weakened, skipped or fixed here. The 61 rows are Appendix A of the rev-7 design (the catalog the coordinator attaches); an entry that cannot open it stops. Reference files on the coordinator's machine (a worker that cannot open them stops): the final design text (/Users/joe/git/phonUX.worktrees/_goalfrontpage-workflow/DESIGN-phonux-0.2-rev7.md: its OWNER ANSWERS block, sections 0, 3.1, 6, 10, 11 and 12 and 'What changed in revision 7') and the catalog JSON (/Users/joe/git/phonUX.worktrees/_goalfrontpage-workflow/DESIGN-phonux-0.2-rev7-catalog.json, also the design's Appendix A, whose default wording carries Q20 and Q21).
2. Class table as data, written by hand in test/support/configure-matrix.ts (never derived from the code under test): 4 classes (NOW, NEXT, STATE, SCOPE) x 9 statuses (pending, live, parked, hidden, protected, closed declared, closed anonymous, undo window, same turn) = 36 cells, each the expected outcome with side effects (is-pending, busy, is-closed, stored-on-record, applied-now, evict calls, unmounts, projection); the design's 7.6 defaults (P is-pending, F busy, A is-closed, C and U stored) are the table's literal rows. executed class cells === 36 (asserted); the DESIGN.md class column is asserted by the resolver entry's drift test and again by the rc entry.
3. One probe per catalog name (61 test calls, each named matrix: followed by the catalog name): it changes the setting on a live panel through the real manager and asserts the before and after observable the row's on_configure cell names (the first sentence after its class word: a button appears, a History row leaves, the z-index changes, close answers not-closable, nothing parks, nothing moves, the next hide uses the new value, the next show uses the new placement), plus the status exceptions the row lists; the loop asserts executed probes === 61 and every name has exactly one probe; for the ten keys whose instance-door call is wrong-scope (three template keys, seven workspace-only tuning keys) every status asserts that refusal.
4. Edge scroll rows (the matrix entry's own cases for the two Q19 rows): scrollOnDrag edge to none on a live panel: the next held drag at the window edge leaves scrollLeft unchanged and, set back to edge, scrolls again; a drag already in flight keeps the mode it began with; nothing scrolls at configure; tuning.edgeScroll zonePx from the default to a quarter of it through configure(id): a pointer at half the default zonePx from the edge (both read from LIBRARY_TUNING_DEFAULTS.edgeScroll) scrolls under the default and not after the change (positive control), configureTuning sets the default for panels without a leaf, null removes the leaf; a hidden or parked panel stores both.
5. Root column for the keys C5 forces (closable, parkable, draggable, placeable, resizable): each asserts settings-conflict on the root (the result's setting is the asked key and its message names rule C5). pinningWhenNotLive expanded over its four values and the per-status object for parked, hidden, closed declared and undo window (the same cells as the pin entry, reached through configure(parkable)).
6. Owner's literal transition cases A: closable true->false on a live panel (control gone, close refused not-closable, History trash gone, clearable re-derived false); inside a declared panel's window (stored, undo restores it unclosable); during a DragToClose exit (release at +epsilon and at +exit end: snaps back, closing.current clears, never stranded); on the root (settings-conflict). parkable on a live phone panel true->false (listed, draggable, placeable, clearable re-derive false at the commit unless named, the settled row within capacity, an instance resizable:true is settings-conflict, a measured row holding a park panel accepts it with no refusal for room); on a tablet panel (tier-not-allowed); false->true on a live fixed column.
7. Owner's literal transition cases B: placeOnShow changed on a hidden panel (no move, no reveal, the next show uses it); placeOnAdd and placeOnReturn on a live panel (no move); hideStickiness sticky->until-room on a hidden panel (still hidden, hold unchanged) and on a live one (the next hide uses it); capacityReturn auto->manual on a stacked panel (stays on the stack, no reveal); overflow park->scroll on a live park panel (nothing parks or returns now; the next opening does not park it) and scroll->park (eligible only at the next overflow); scrollOnOpen none on a live panel (the next open does not scroll); scrollOnMove follow->none (the next pin does not scroll; nothing scrolls at configure); keepMounted true on a previously live, unmounted parked panel (no mount until its next show and park), true->false on a hidden kept panel (unmounts in the commit's layout effect, picture and viewState untouched), true during a park exit, true beside viewState reset-on-park (settings-conflict); viewState keep->reset-on-park on a parked panel and the reverse.
8. Protected (T) and side effects with spies: evictionRank and evictionOrder skip an acted-on panel; hideWhenLast with an acted-on panel; resetLayout tier growth in a park row; snapshot 'never' evicts the stored picture once from a layout effect (a spy on the updater sees none and StrictMode's double updater still evicts once); keepMounted false unmounts in the effect; persist changes only the next projection; rowGap re-prices; every effect named in a probe's side-effects field is asserted equal.
9. S7 sources for the release gate: test/settings-coverage-sources.test.ts greps test/*.test.ts for the literal `differential('NAME'` for every catalog name and asserts each name is found in exactly one file whose basename is one of the owning entries' differential files: settings-enforcement-permissions, settings-enforcement-visibility, settings-enforcement-motion, panel-placement, settings-enforcement-tuning, keep-mounted, settings-enforcement-overflow, settings-enforcement-scroll, settings-enforcement-edge-scroll, settings-enforcement-memory, workspace-attach (the afterClose differential, named by the attach entry), panel-manager-operations, panel-pin, header-buttons (each followed by .test.ts) and that every name has a `matrix:NAME` probe; mutant: delete one differential call; red.

### 20. app-real-layout-gate (phonux-app)

**Real-layout gate: keepMounted, the scrolling row, launch at the far end, edge scrolling, rowGap, header buttons and the toast in the app's Electron harness**

Intent: Prove keepMounted, the scrolling row, launch at the far end (initialScroll 'end'), edge scrolling while dragging, rowGap, header buttons and the toast in real layout: a test-only harness page (its own build input; desktop.tsx and main.ts untouched) on the packed library build measures cost, focus, motion, capture, scroll, drags, spacing and buttons and commits the numbers. Scratch profile only.

Prerequisites: phonux-configure-matrix (cross-repo)

Touches:
- electron.vite.config.ts
- client/render-gate.html
- client/render-gate/main.tsx
- client/render-gate/RenderGateHarness.tsx
- test/phonux-render-gate-electron.test.ts
- test/support/render-gate-views.tsx
- test/render-gate-measurements.json

Evidence required:
1. Preconditions, each asserted. Cross-repo prerequisite: when it files this entry the coordinator writes the library worktree path and its commit LIBCOMMIT into the issue (phonux-configure-matrix merged there first); the test reads the path from the environment variable PHONUX_LIB_DIR and stops unless git -C of that path prints LIBCOMMIT. The library worktree must be at or after 7a60e82 (`git -C PATH merge-base --is-ancestor 7a60e82 LIBCOMMIT` exits 0), and the coordinator writes LIBCOMMIT and PATH into the issue. The `phonux/testing` alias maps to the packed fakeHost.js, whose export is createFakeHost (the 22-alias removal dropped createFakePhoneAPIs), so the harness code uses createFakeHost and the new names only. The 0.2 build never enters the app's node_modules: the library is built from that worktree after rm -rf dist (npm run build, test -f dist/package.json), npm pack is run INSIDE its dist/ into SCRATCH (the root package is private and points at .ts) and the tarball is extracted to SCRATCH/pack/package, which gets one symlink, SCRATCH/pack/package/node_modules, to the app's node_modules so the packed build finds react, MUI and motion there (one copy of each); a tar listing grep for inert in PanelRow.js and for computeRowNumbers in the index are each at least 1. No npm install runs: node_modules/phonux/package.json prints version 0.1.0 before and after the run and `git status --porcelain package.json package-lock.json` prints nothing, so test/phone-api-layer.test.ts (it asserts the 22 aliases that 0.2 removes) and typecheck:app stay green until app-adopt-phonux-0.2-imports. The measurements JSON records libCommit (that rev-parse) as its first field. BASE for the app's test count before any edit: `SCRATCH=$(mktemp -d); npm test 2>&1 | tee $SCRATCH/t.log; awk '/^ℹ tests [0-9]+$/{n=$3} END{if(n==""||n==0)exit 1; print n}' $SCRATCH/t.log`. Groups sharing one Electron run: A keepMounted, B row gap, C scrolling row and drag, D header buttons, E toast.
2. Isolation: one Electron process, a mkdtemp --user-data-dir, CDP port range 10660-10699 (a grep over test/ shows no overlap with the spans of 40 starting at 9440, 10400, 10500, 10540, 10580 and 10620, each listed), acquireExclusiveDesktopBuildLock held for the file's life, and the real sessions.json (~/Library/Application Support/simplebrowser/sessions/sessions.json) sha256 before == after (control: an unreadable path fails the file); the build is `electron-vite build --mode test` with PHONUX_RENDER_GATE=1 and PHONUX_PACK_DIR set to SCRATCH/pack/package: electron.vite.config.ts adds client/render-gate.html as a second renderer input and aliases phonux and phonux/testing to PHONUX_PACK_DIR's index.js and fakeHost.js ONLY when PHONUX_RENDER_GATE is set, and throws only when PHONUX_RENDER_GATE is set and PHONUX_PACK_DIR is unset or lacks index.js or fakeHost.js, so a gate build can never silently bundle 0.1.0; every other build (a production build and the plain `--mode test` builds, which keep MODE 'test' for the capture controller's hook) is unchanged. Five existing Electron tests build with `npm run build:desktop -- --mode test` and no PHONUX_PACK_DIR and stay green and unedited: test/panel-anchor-placement-electron.test.ts, test/per-view-capture-integration.test.ts, test/workspace-state-persistence-electron.test.ts, test/panel-form-factors-electron.test.ts and test/panel-spawn-permanent-columns-electron.test.ts (`git diff --name-only` lists none of them; they run in the full npm test with PHONUX_LIB_DIR unset). An error the app's own desktop.html logs before the navigation is recorded and does not fail the file, an error on the harness page does; the test connects to the app's desktop.html target and navigates that window to the harness page by CDP Page.navigate, so electron/main.ts and desktop.tsx stay untouched; `grep -rl render-gate out/ | wc -l` prints 0 after a production build and 0 after a plain `--mode test` build with neither variable set (each build empties out/ first), and at least 1 after the gate build (PHONUX_RENDER_GATE=1).
3. Ran-at-all controls and layout cost (A): the baseline run (zero kept views, three panels) returns container clientWidth > 0, a row rect width > 0 and a probe-view DOM count equal to the number mounted; every later number is compared with this baseline, never with a literal 0. With 0, 1, 4 and 12 parked kept tall probe views (3000 px high, 2000 px wide content) the container's clientWidth, scrollWidth, scrollHeight, the row's rect and the ResizeObserver callback count during park and hide equal the baseline, while document.querySelectorAll('[data-keep-probe]').length equals the number mounted and getComputedStyle(slot).display is 'none' for each. A live panel scrolled out of view is mounted and not display none.
4. Focus, access, state and memory (A): after parking, probe input.focus() leaves document.activeElement outside the probe; 25 Tab presses never land inside (the sequence is recorded); elementFromPoint at the former position is not inside; CDP Accessibility.getFullAXTree has no node named by the probe's input label; during the exit slide the slot is inert. Exact (asserted equal to the committed file): an input value, a React counter, a canvas pixel and an iframe's counter surviving 5 park/show cycles, scrollTop of an overflow child, DOM-node counts; recorded only: a CSS animation's progress, timer ticks while hidden, heap numbers. usePanelHidden is true while hidden and false after the revive. CDP Memory.getDOMCounters: 20 kept hidden probes add 20 x nodes-per-view (+-2%) and closing them returns to the baseline +-1%.
5. Motion and capture (A): requestAnimationFrame samples of the slot's rect through a revive from a left park to a right enter and from a right park to a right enter: the first sample is within 2 px of rest + width + gap on the enter side, x decreases monotonically to rest, opacity rises 0 to 1, total time within 100 ms of tuning.slide.durationSec (0.4 default; a second run with 0.8 set through configure); an exit samples monotonically to its target then computes to display none; animated:false and an unchanged-target park reach display none within one frame. Capture with the app's real controller: a probe Panel with a viewId, parked: a picture is stored with width equal to the probe's clientWidth; a capture asked while hidden leaves capturedAt unchanged (control: the same call on a visible probe changes it); after the revive a re-park stores a new picture.
6. Scrolling row (C), container 1000 px, History, 5 pinned-left and 5 web panels, all scroll: container scrollWidth 4482 > clientWidth and computed overflow-x is auto; no panel has live:false; at scrollLeft 0 the first panel's rect.left equals the container's rect.left + rowGap (+-1 px: no blank margin) and the last is not inside; at max scrollLeft the last panel's rect.right + rowGap is within 1 px of the container's rect.right. Opening a web panel while scrolled to 0: after the glide the new panel's rect lies inside the container (+-1 px) and the glide time is within 100 ms of tuning.slide.durationSec; with scrollOnOpen 'none' scrollLeft is unchanged (control: the probe template sets scrollOnOpen explicitly, 'into-view' then 'none', so the gate does not depend on the library default); a History click on a live out-of-view panel scrolls it into view.
7. Gesture, glide and landing (C): a 30-event wheel burst with a momentum tail over 600 ms under rowGesture step ends exactly on the next panel start (+-1 px) with no mid-panel rest, a second burst inside 350 ms does nothing; 10 ArrowRight repeats (key auto-repeat) advance 10 stops; tabbing into an out-of-view panel scrolls it into view natively (activeElement inside the container rect); under 'free' the scrollbar exists (offsetHeight above clientHeight on the scroller) and under 'step' it does not. initialScroll 'root' on the eleven-panel row of bullet 6 (maximum scrollLeft 3482), with a REAL saved file of its 5 pinned and 5 web panels (storage load delayed by 300 ms): at 100 ms scrollLeft is 0, after the restore History's rect.left is within 1 px of the window start (scrollLeft 2030, below the maximum) and a wheel before the restore makes it skip. Launch at the far end (the value phonux-app ships, owner Q20, passed literally by the harness page as `tuning: { initialScroll: 'end' }` on the same eleven-panel row and the same REAL saved file with storage load delayed 300 ms; inside this group's test, no new test() call, so the count of 13 stands): at 100 ms scrollLeft is 0; after the restore scrollLeft equals the farthest legal value measured in the page (scrollWidth minus clientWidth, 3482 in the 1000 px container) within 1 px; the last web panel's rect.right plus rowGap is within 1 px of the container's right edge; History's rect.right and every pinned panel's lie left of the container's left edge; a wheel before the restore makes the launch scroll skip; a second harness page with the library default (no tuning) stays at scrollLeft 0 (control).
8. Edge scrolling in real layout (C), CDP Input.dispatchMouseEvent, with zonePx, maxPxPerSec and reducedStepMs read from the packed build's exported LIBRARY_TUNING_DEFAULTS.edgeScroll (48, 900 and 500 today, in that order) and never typed: (a) speed: a press on a web panel's grab bar, a 40 px move right and a hold 8 px inside the container's right edge for 1000 ms: scrollLeft grows monotonically at a mean speed within 15 percent of maxPxPerSec x (zonePx - 8) / zonePx (750 px/s today); the dragged panel's rect centre stays within 2 px of the pointer x at every sampled frame. (b) The strip's end, the runaway probe: with eleven panels, hold the pointer on the container's right edge (depth 1) until scrollLeft is sampled at the maximum, with a ceiling computed in the test as 2 x maxScroll / (maxPxPerSec x (zonePx - 1) / zonePx) x 1000 ms from the LIBRARY_TUNING_DEFAULTS values it already reads (at depth 1 the speed is maxPxPerSec x (zonePx - 1) / zonePx, about 881 px/s today, so the 3482 px take about 4 s and the ceiling is about 8000 ms today; a fixed 3000 ms would stop short, and a changed default is a one-value edit), then keep holding 500 ms more: scrollLeft stays at the maximum within 1 px (the maximum is computed in the test from the strip: the last panel's rest rect.right plus rowGap minus the container's right edge, measured before the drag), never exceeds it by more than 1 px, and the container's scrollWidth sampled every frame, through the settle too, equals its value before the drag (the dragged box does not grow it). (c) Landing: hold at depth 1 until the sampled scroll distance S first reaches 2 x maxPxPerSec (1800 today), then release: after the settle the dragged panel's rect.left equals its new slot's rest within 1 px, the hop count equals the hop rule's count for the released offset (literal computed in the test from the sampled S and the neighbour spans), and between consecutive sampled frames after release the box's rect.left never changes by more than the pointer offset. (d) A vertical drag with the same hold leaves scrollLeft unchanged. (e) Under Emulation.setEmulatedMedia reduced motion the row jumps by whole panel starts, one per reducedStepMs, and never rests between starts. (f) A hold at the container's middle scrolls nothing (control). (g) On a second harness page with rowGesture 'off' the same hold still scrolls (the scroller is found by its mark), and a native wheel burst during the hold changes nothing under 'step' and 'off'.
9. Move, resize and exit (C): with the pinned panel at scrollLeft 3482 (last of eleven), a pin press scrolls the row so the pressed panel is inside the container (scrollLeft 0 after the glide), keeps the focused pin button inside the viewport and document.activeElement stays that button; unpin returns it and the row follows (scrollLeft 3482); a size press to tablet keeps the panel whole in view; with scrollOnMove 'none' scrollLeft is unchanged on each (control: the probe template sets scrollOnMove 'follow' explicitly, so the gate does not depend on the library default, and follow changes it). hide() of the first web panel (rest 2436) samples its x between 2436 and 2030 only (never left of 2030) and ends display none; the same hide in a legacy-row harness page exits toward -ownSpan as before.
10. Row gap in real layout (B): rowGap 0, 16, 40 and 64 set through the workspace: adjacent live slots' rect distance equals rowGap, the container's padding-left equals rowGap, and the park-mode live count equals the count computed in the test from computeRowLayout with the same gap and container width (a table of four literals); at gap 40 usePanelReorderSteps returns one hop for a drag of 0.6 of the neighbour's span and none for 0.4.

### 23. app-adopt-phonux-0.2-workspace (phonux-app)

**Adopt the phonux workspace in the app: History and web templates, header-button slots, tuning sites, the scrolling row, launch at the far end, the fixed toast, SPEC and README**

Intent: The app adopts the workspace through the public API: History is the root template, web a multiple template declaring size and pin as header-button slots, a module-scope createWorkspace replaces initialEntries and storage, the row scrolls and launches at the far end (initialScroll 'end'), tuning sites read resolved values, the toast is fixed, SPEC and README reworded.

Prerequisites: app-adopt-phonux-0.2-imports

Touches:
- client/src/desktop/**
- electron/main.ts
- SPEC.md
- README.md
- tools/file-size-baseline.json
- plans/phonux-0.2-retargets.txt
- test/fixtures/live-panel-actions.html

- test/phonux-render-gate-electron.test.ts
- test/app-launch-position.test.ts

A History row click on a hidden panel still uses the activate path and scrolls the row to it; the Fab's panels.create makes a web instance with the template's settings (a mount test asserts closable true and listed true) and the row scrolls to it (expected under the same read of LIBRARY_PANEL_DEFAULTS.scrollOnOpen).

Launch position (owner Q20, 'Right end, newest first': the newest closable panels are in view at launch; the pinned panels and History start off screen to the left). client/src/desktop/workspace/appWorkspace.ts exports `APP_INITIAL_SCROLL = 'end'`, a literal that is not read from the library default (which stays 'start'), and the app's createWorkspace call passes `tuning: { initialScroll: APP_INITIAL_SCROLL }`. (1) Options test, in test/app-launch-position.test.ts: the workspace handle built by the app's module has `workspace.tuning.initialScroll === 'end'` (a literal assertion) while a bare createWorkspace() reads 'start'; mutant: the constant changed to 'root' turns it red. (2) Mounted launch, in the same file: GoalFrontPage mounted over a saved file of 5 pinned panels and 5 web panels (eleven with History; container geometry stubbed at clientWidth 1000 as the row-numbers tests do: stripWidth 4450, windowWidth 968, rowMarginLeft 0, farthest scrollLeft 3482 read from panels.rowNumbers): while only History exists scrollLeft is 0 and no scroll was requested; after the restore settles scrollLeft is exactly 3482; History (left edge 16 + 5 x 406 = 2046, right edge 2436) and every pinned panel lie wholly left of scrollLeft; the two newest web panels (left edges 3670 and 4076, right edge 4466) lie wholly inside [3482, 4482]; a wheel before the restore settles makes the launch scroll skip. 8. SPEC.md changes only sections 1.1, 2.1, 3.1, 3.3, 3.4, 3.5, 3.7, 5.3, 5.4 and 7.2, through SPEC section 9's path (1/2); section 6.1 is the retire entry's and stays as it left it. 1.1: 'The app has one screen: a single horizontal row of columns, centred in the window while it fits and scrolling horizontally when it does not. When the app opens the row is scrolled to its right end, so the newest closable panels are in view and the pinned panels and History start off screen to the left.' 2.1 appends: 'History may scroll out of view like any other column.' 3.1: 'Every panel is in exactly one state at a time: live (open in the row, in view or scrolled out of view), parked (listed in History only), or closed (gone from both).' 3.3: 'The row scrolls instead of hiding panels: more panels can be open than are in view.' 3.4: 'Opening or showing a panel scrolls the row to it, by the least distance that shows it whole. Pinning, unpinning or resizing a panel that is in view keeps it in view the same way.'
9. SPEC.md wording (2/2). 3.5: 'The row gesture (a horizontal swipe, Shift with the wheel, or the left and right arrow keys) scrolls the row one panel toward that side, and shows a hidden panel that lies that way. With nothing further that way it does nothing.' (the library default revealOnScroll 'edges' is not overridden by the app, so the gesture still reaches a hidden panel; a mount test in the rewritten gesture tests of test/row-scroll-by-hiding.test.ts asserts it) 3.7: 'For 10 seconds after the most recent close, Undo is offered. Undo returns every panel closed in that window to its old place, live if it was live, scrolls the row to the last one restored, and removes its log entry.' 5.3: 'Either is narrowed only as far as the window, and never below phone width.' 5.4: 'Changing a panel's form factor resizes it in place: it stays live, keeps its content and stays in view. No other panel is parked.' 7.2: 'phonux hands a view its panel as a summary only: id, url, title, whether it is live, whether it is locked against closing, its opaque params, and its form factor and size. It never passes the view the app's internal panel record, such as anchors or the flags that decide parking. A view declared as a template also receives a handle to that one panel and a read-only reader of the panel list; neither can make a panel unclosable or change another panel's settings.'
